# Package research and quality audit (batch 132)

One row per package, lowest-level packages first, written by the helper that audited it (never its author). "Sources
read in full" names only papers and specs fetched and read end to end; anything read as an abstract, behind a paywall or
from memory is listed under "open". Losses stay in. Each row's commit hash is in `git log --grep="(<package>)"`.

## Where this stands (2026-11-10)

102 package folders. 99 have at least one audit row below; 19 of those (blender, cinematic, decoders, gltf, loaders,
materials, mixer, netcode, procedural, react, save, text, tiles, toon, video, voxel-edit, voxel-pathtracer,
wfc, xr) have two rows (voxel-pathtracer three), from two machines or a later pass, and all are kept.
One row says "partial": voxel-pathtracer's first pass, whose owed files the vpt-b and vpt-c passes below it read
line by line; ifc's gap table is fully matched. The 3 packages with no audit,
physics, physics2d and ragdoll, are excluded by Scott's physics hold and listed at
the end of the table. Every source a row could not read in full (paywall,
dead link, a PDF with no extractable text, an ISO licence page we did not accept) is named in that row and in
the finish-later list. Each row's regression tests fail on the code before its fix unless the
row says otherwise. Correctness review of the work landed on 2026-11-10 is in
the review 5 list; what our checks caught and what we got wrong that day is in
[perf-history.md](perf-history.md).

| dof | Demers, "Depth of Field: A Survey of Techniques", GPU Gems ch. 23 (2004) | CoC against the sensor-plane thin-lens form (signs, pixel scale H / (2 f tan(fov / 2)), maxRadius clamp in pixels); reversed-Z linearisation (d = 1 near, 0 far) and the orthographic mix; golden angle 2.39996323 = pi (3 - sqrt 5); tap tiers 2.26 / 3.19 px = sqrt(16 / pi), sqrt(32 / pi) (one tap per disk pixel); filmGauge focal length tan(fov / 2) = gauge / (2 f); CPU tests 22/22; Metal GPU lens check and WebGL2-compat check pass | Option doc and README said temporal DOF is WebGPU only and throws on the WebGL2 layer, while it runs there (temporal-compat test, compat GPU check); README claimed "about a quarter of the cost", never timed | Both doc claims corrected | Potmesil & Chakravarty 1981 (ACM paywall), Vogel 1979 (Elsevier paywall), Eldar et al. 1997 (IEEE paywall) and Hecht, Optics (book) not read in full. CoC uses the sensor-at-f convention, which overstates blur by S1 / (S1 - f) against the exact angular form (1% at defaults, large in macro); kept as the published convention, noted in clean-room D-02. Depth tolerances (1% / 0.001 near-far test, 5% coarse reuse) are Margo choices with no measurement on record |
| splines | None in full: Yuksel, Schaefer and Keyser 2011 manuscript fetched but its PDF text could not be extracted here; Wang et al. 2008, Kochanek and Bartels 1984, Schneider 1990 paywalled or in books | Centripetal, chordal and uniform Catmull-Rom tangents (Barry-Goldman form, Bezier conversion b1 = p1 + m1 / 3); Kochanek-Bartels source and destination weights; uniform B-spline basis; double reflection steps; closed-loop twist; offset error bound (Bernstein hull of the residual and of the unnormalised normal, interval division); fitSpline tolerance probed against a 200,000-sample scan (holds); every option has its default; CPU tests 34/34; dependent cinematic 122/122 | closestPointOnSpline searched by sampling then bisecting around the best sample: on a 167-piece fitted track at the default 256 samples it returned distances up to 0.70 where the true distance is under 0.05 (14x); tests passed only by raising samples to 2,000-4,000. Clean-room had no record for Kochanek-Bartels, the B-spline basis, double reflection or the fit, and four merge-duplicated copies of the batch 225 paragraph. Spline.tangent is a central difference (h = 1e-5) though the exact derivative exists: 3.4e-6 direction error at 6.4e6 m coordinates | Exact closest point: quintic stationary points per Bezier piece on its Bernstein form, convex-hull pruning, regression test (fitted track and a helix seeded by 2 samples). Sources recorded, duplicates removed | Spline.tangent finite difference (kept: SplineFrames and cinematic read it; small error measured). Errors are plain RangeError and TypeError without help links (no shared rangeError helper or errors-page entry) |

Eberly, "Triangulation by Ear Clipping" (Geometric Tools, 2002, PDF, 6 pages) | Part 1 (de23d50d6): optimizeVertexCache against Forsyth (32-entry LRU growing by 3, last-triangle score 0.75, decay (1 - (i - 3) / 29)^1.5, valence boost 2 n^-0.5, -1 for a vertex with no triangles left); Delaunay float filters (3 + 16 eps) eps and (10 + 96 eps) eps with eps = 2^-53, exact BigInt fallback; Loop beta (5/8 - (3/8 + cos(2 pi / n) / 4)^2) / n. Part 2 (this pass): hole bridging against Eberly section 5 (ray to +x from the hole's rightmost vertex, nearest hit, the edge's larger-x end, then the vertex inside triangle M I P of smallest angle; the code takes every vertex in the triangle, not only reflex ones, which picks the same visible vertex because a blocking edge must have an end of smaller angle inside the triangle); property runs written for this audit, each against an independent reference: 20,000 random star polygons with up to 3 holes, triangle areas sum to the polygon's area to 1e-9 with no clockwise triangle; quickhull on 1,800 point sets (ball, sphere, integer grids with coplanar faces, a coordinate offset of 1e6, a 16-gon prism) closed two-manifold with every input point inside every face to 1e-8 of the span; NurbsCurve against a separate Cox-de Boor recursion on 400 random rational curves of degree 1 to 4 with repeated knots (4e-16) and first derivatives against central differences (1e-9 relative); nurbsCircle radius error 9e-16; knot insertion and degree elevation keep the curve to 9e-16; sweepGeometry volume against Pappus on a helix (exact at 400 steps, also with scale and up), a straight loft against the frustum formula; tessellateGeometry keeps area and stays crack-free on a sphere, a box and a torus knot at three limits; decalGeometry area exactly the box footprint on a plane under three turns and a scaled matrix, UVs 0 to 1; the four Platonic solids' volumes and circumradii; extrudeGeometry volume with a hole, with and without a one-step bevel (22 and 30.36, the prismatoid values); flowGeometry normals against the bent mesh's face normals (cos above 0.999 at the documented vertex density); MeshSurfaceSampler's uniform sqrt draw and its Dirichlet(2, 1, 1) vertex-weight mix checked by derivation. CPU tests 332/332; Metal GPU check passes (the check has no WebGL2 mode: GeometryGenerator needs WebGPU and throws a coded error with a help link there) | Nothing wrong in the parts checked. A mesh stretched 25 times along a tight helix with flowGeometry fit folds its long thin triangles (cos -1 against the bent face): the documented limit (only vertices move, so the mesh needs vertices along the axis), not a defect | None needed | Shewchuk 1997 PDF text now extracts with pdftotext, but its error-bound tables (Tables 1 and 5) are set in a symbol font and come out blank, so the two bounds still rest on memory. the browser test's reference had the same omission | Each term clamped in the shader; stereo-browser.mjs reference follows Dubois 2009 and fails on the old shader | Kooima assumes a rectangular screen; offAxisFrustum accepts skewed corners without a word. The default circle of confusion is filmGauge / 1500 (0.024 mm), a convention, not from a source read here. Not read: Potmesil and Chakravarty 1981 (paywalled), Bourke's cube and sphere notes (web pages, not re-read), Thorne's floating origin thesis. No mutation score |
| studio (was staging) | None read in full in this pass: the clean-room rows name Sloan 2008 (Stupid SH Tricks), Jensen 1996 (photon maps), Wyman and Davis 2006, Halton 1960 and PBR 3rd/4th ed., which were not re-fetched | Contact shadow: orthographic nearness pass, Gaussian sigma = radius / 2 cut at 3 sigma (two-sided tail 0.27%), bilinear pair offsets (k a + (k + 1) b) / (a + b); AccumulatedShadows: planar projection from a disk light rederived (x' = (H x - x_l y) / (H - y), depth (H - h) y / (h (H - y)) over 0..h), disk radius R sqrt(u) with u the base-2 radical inverse and golden-angle azimuth; AmbientShadows: Halton bases 2, 3, 5 for light pick, radius and angle, Malley cosine hemisphere (r, sqrt(1 - r^2)), the directional projection x - (d_x / d_y) y; GroundShadows: dominant light from the luminance-weighted first SH band in Margo's (y, z, x) order and the inverse of environment's yaw (checked against ibl.ts); caustics: unpolarised Fresnel rs and rp, Snell vector refraction eta d + (eta cos i - cos t) n, front-face eta = 1 / ior, photon flux cos / N with the footprint cancelling against the texel count, bilinear splat and [1 2 1]^2 / 16 reconstruction, radiance E / pi; ProgressiveStill and TexturedStill are wrappers over voxel-pathtracer (audited with that package); CPU 48/48; test:gpu chain (browser-check, ground x3, accumulate, pathtrace, orientation, floor, hdr-shadow, the compat variants), materials, ambient, caustics and backdrop pass on Metal WebGPU and with their compat runs | Mac sweep: test:gpu stopped in pathtrace-browser.mjs's TAA step with 'TAA velocityOnly at 4x MSAA reads the scene target' (no velocityOnly set): materials' native glass, new today, swapped c.color at order -64 after TAA's capture at -70, so TAA lost its scene target on any native-glass glTF (the flight helmet). browser-check.mjs under MARGO_COMPAT=1 needs timestamp-query, which the WebGL2 layer lacks: a timing check, not run in compat (its siblings have their own --compat runs) | Native glass fix landed in materials (6c0d11cd6), regression rows in materials/test/native-glass-browser.mjs; staging needed no change | Wide blurs (radius over about 43 texels, where 3 sigma passes 64 taps per side) space their taps more than a texel apart, so the bilinear pair is no longer exact and the kernel undersamples; accumulated and ambient shadows weight visibility equally (stated, not radiometric); GroundShadows' environment yaw sign is pinned only at rotation pi in tests, not at pi / 2. No mutation score 2026-11-16: `AccumulatedShadows` gains `temporal` and `blend` (drei AccumulativeShadows modes; Heckbert and Herf 1997, CMU-CS-97-104, read), accumulate.test.js and accumulate-browser.mjs. 2026-11-16: an `AmbientShadows` light's `mapSize` lookup is 2 x 2 percentage-closer filtering (the four nearest texel centres' shadow tests, weighted bilinearly, clamped at the map edge) from Reeves, Salesin and Cook, "Rendering Antialiased Shadows with Depth Maps", SIGGRAPH 1987, https://doi.org/10.1145/37402.37435, not read (the publisher and author copies did not load; built from the method's standard description); accumulate-ambient-browser.mjs checks the ramp 2 max(0, 1 - |x - 0.5|) on both GPU backends, which a nearest lookup misses by 0.94 |

its Eq. 9 writes e^(-s^2 / v) for its own Gaussian, and with d'Eon and Luebke's G the slab integral is e^(-s^2 / 2v)); Babylon's PBRSubSurfaceConfiguration translucency properties from its published type declarations (descriptions only) | Hashed alpha: two power-of-two grids, x = mix by fract(log2), a = min(t, 1 - t) and the three-piece CDF (x^2 / 2a(1 - a), (x - a / 2) / (1 - a), 1 - (1 - x)^2 / 2a(1 - a)) against Listing 1 and Eq. 1, clamp to [1e-6, 1]; the scale option is the reciprocal of the paper's g_HashScale (documented as cells per pixel). Skin: G(v, r) = e^(-r^2 / 2v) / (2 pi v) as the chapter defines it; Penner's pre-integration D(theta, r) with a cubic sample spacing and its u^2 Jacobian; 14 mm cut-off = 5 sigma of the 7.41 mm^2 Gaussian; curvature in 1 / mm from |dN| / |dP| and unitsPerMeter; F0 0.028 for index 1.4. Stochastic tiling: the skew (1, -0.57735027; 0, 1.15470054) and both triangles' vertex-weight pairing against Heitz and Neyret's grid, the variance-preserving gain 1 / sqrt(sum w^2) about the 1 x 1 mip mean. Hair: the lobe cos^p normalised by sqrt(p / 2 pi) is a Gaussian of sigma^2 = 1 / p normalised over the angle, TRT width 2x is p / 4, F0 0.0465 for 1.55. Deviation kept and documented: lobes use separable Smith G1 terms where the anisotropy README's sample uses the height-correlated V, so the anisotropic lobe reduces to the base shader's isotropic one. the sRGB encode (12.92 below 0.0031308, 1.055 x^(1 / 2.4) - 0.055); the additive (src-alpha, one) and multiply (zero, src) blend factors; gltf/pbr's dielectric F0 min(((ior - 1) / (ior + 1))^2 specularColor, 1) x specular against the specular README; mix(min, max, g) thin-film thickness against the iridescence README; a thin cornea of parallel faces refracts as air into the aqueous humour (Snell across two interfaces); the surface and feature uniform layouts against their setters; fifth pass (2026-11-14 and 15): stochastic.ts against Heitz and Neyret (the skew, both triangles' vertex-weight pairing, the variance gain about the 1 x 1 mip) and Mikkelsen 2022 (sharpened weights by a power, renormalised); rival check against three.js MeshPhysicalMaterial, Babylon PBR and PlayCanvas StandardMaterial: iridescence (the same Belcour and Barla film with the KHR_materials_iridescence factor, IOR, thickness range and both maps, in direct and ambient light) and glass (refraction blurred by roughness, volume attenuation, dispersion, on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer) match; skin (pre-integrated, with `@margoengine/post/sss` for the screen-space blur Babylon's subsurface does) has no rival in three.js or PlayCanvas; skin with Babylon's translucency for thin backlit parts was a gap: skin with `subsurface` added the generic wrap on top of the skin table (light past the terminator counted twice) and let light through by a flat tint, so `skin({ thickness })` now passes it by the skin profile integrated over a slab's back plane (Jimenez, Whelan, Sundstedt and Gutierrez 2010), checked against the integral on the CPU and within one 8-bit step at 2 and 4 mm on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer | Native glass (order -64) redirected c.color after TAA's capture (order -70) had taken it, so TAA threw 'TAA velocityOnly at 4x MSAA reads the scene target' for any glTF with native glass (found by staging's pathtrace browser test on the Mac); second pass: Transmission's scene-copy mip chain took a 2x2 box from texel 2p, so at an odd size the last column and row never reached the coarser levels and each level drifted up to a texel (37 x 23: level 1 was 106x the half-float tolerance off an area filter); its comment said the odd edge repeated. test/physical.test.js's 'group 0 binds the volume' matched the old reduce bind groups (source and sampler), not the volume; third pass (2026-11-14): atlas.ts remapTileUVs measured v from the source image's top row for every grid, but a drawable tileset (image, bitmap, canvas) is uploaded with flipY, top row at v = 1, and padTileGrid stores it bottom row first, so its UVs landed on the wrong tile rows and upside down inside the tile (a 3 x 3 grid of 4 x 2 tiles: every texel but the middle row's missed); serialization.ts restored a captured drawable's top-row-first pixels as raw data, which a Texture keeps at v = 0, so a flipY texture came back upside down, and read an image element at its layout width and height, not naturalWidth and naturalHeight; batched.ts setMatrix accepted a finite double past float32's range (1e39), which the Float32Array stored as Infinity, so the item drew from Infinity and NaN; fourth pass: padTileGrid capped only the output area, so one row of 420 tiles of 16 x 8 (padding 2) made an 8,400 x 12 image past the 8,192 maxTextureDimension2D; clone() shared the feature list without joining it, so an eye's user count missed the clone (the clone dropping the eye destroyed the original's uniform buffer) and alphaHash left its raised alphaTest (0.5) on a clone that dropped it; copy() spliced into the frozen feature list and threw TypeError for any material with features; specularColor [2, 4, 25] threw RangeError though KHR_materials_specular has no maximum; iridescenceThicknessRange [400, 100] threw though the minimum MAY exceed the maximum; clippingPlanes [[1e-30, 0, 0, 1e10]] normalised d to 1e40, Infinity in the float32 uniform; a NaN tangent (or tangent w) packed as no tangent instead of refusing; the eye bent its view and light rays by the cornea's 1.376, where a thin cornea leaves them as air into the aqueous humour (1.336); fifth pass: Material, materialLobes and transmission refused IOR 0, KHR_materials_ior's specular-glossiness mode, and the shader paths read 0 as IOR 1; animation's pointer player wrote IOR samples into a material whose file IOR is 0 and took keys below 1; createMesh made the scene's draw queue before runtime.use(model), so a first mesh whose model failed to register left an empty queue pass behind | 6c0d11cd6: glass reads the owning capture's resolved color and leaves the target alone; TAA rows in test/native-glass-browser.mjs fail before and pass after; second pass: the chain takes shared/mipmaps.js's area filter (regression: the 37 x 23 readback in test/transmission-background-browser.mjs, fails before, passes after, native and compat); the volume assertion now looks for the binding-1 buffer; third pass: TileGridAtlas gains `flipped` and remapTileUVs measures a flipped tile's v from its bottom image row (regression: the last test of test/tile-grid.test.js, fails before); a captured flipY drawable's texture records flipRows and restores its rows reversed, images read at their natural size (regression: the last test of test/serialization.test.js, fails before); setMatrix checks each value as float32 (regression: the 1e39 assertion in tests/batched.test.js, fails before); fourth pass: padTileGrid takes maxSize (default 4096) and wraps the tiles into the most square layout whose edges fit (regression: the long thin grid test of test/tile-grid.test.js, fails before); clone and copy join each feature through its captured state or attach, and copy replaces the frozen list (regression: test/feature-ownership.test.js, three tests, fail before); specularColor takes components >= 0 finite as float32, the thickness range takes either order, a plane's normalised d must be finite as float32, NaN tangents throw (regression: test/spec-ranges.test.js, four tests, fail before); eye gains chamberIor (default 1.336) for the rays and keeps ior for F0 (regression: the aqueous humour test of test/features.test.js); fifth pass: 45a4ea7bd (regression: test/ior-zero.test.js and the IOR 0 test of tests/transmission-ior.test.js, fail before); d6a7a1c30 (regression: packages/animation/tests/ior-pointer.test.js, fails before); 497d5006a (regression: the draw-queue test of test/material-usage.test.js, fails before) | Heitz and Neyret's HPG 2018 paper text is not read (HAL answered with a robot check; the talk slides with notes are); normal maps tile as encoded xyz with the variance gain, where Mikkelsen blends derivatives (limits.md); the hair model is tangent-shifted Kajiya-Kay lobes with Marschner-style normalisation, not Marschner's model (it drops the cos(phi / 2) azimuthal factor and TT); Penner and Borshukov (GPU Pro 2) is a book; the eye bends by the aqueous humour (1.336) and does not model the cornea's two radii. Hashed alpha's shadows cut at `cutoff` where PlayCanvas dithers them (known-bugs); light through thin skin is the sun's only and its depth is painted, where Babylon's translucency takes every light and a thickness map (limits.md). No mutation score |

Second pass (moments, peel, precise, meshes, vfx): moments.ts sums optical depth tau = -log(1 - a) (a clamped to 0.999) times e^(ik phi), k = 1..3, phi = (2 pi - pi / 10)(z + 1) / 2 over the log-warped depth (near -1, far 1 from the uniform 2 / log(far / near) and -1 - x log near); the bias 0.997 moves b1..b3 toward the uniform circle's moments (0); the Toeplitz system G[j][k] = b[k - j] with right side conj(x0)^j is the Lagrange condition of minimising E|p(e^(i phi))|^2 subject to p(x0) = 1, so its kernel polynomial's roots are the canonical representation through x0; the LDL^H factors, forward and back substitution match that system; Cardano with the larger cube candidate, one Newton step and the projection to the circle; the Lagrange weights m3 - (a + b + c) m2 + (ab + ac + bc) m1 - abc over (l - a)(l - b)(l - c); the piecewise psi is continuous and monotonic (-1, 1, 3, 5, 7 at 0, pi/2, pi, 3pi/2, 2pi), x0 counts a quarter and wrapping-zone points in proportion; the bit-level half rounding (add 0xfff plus the kept low bit, mask 0xffffe000, subnormals by 2^-24) is round to nearest even; the depth span from the eight box corners on the camera axis. peel.ts: reversed Z keeps the nearest fragment with clip.z strictly below the previous layer (first layer against 2), opaque depth is applied only at the far-to-near resolve (greater-equal on the layer depth), an empty layer (depth 0) stops every later one, coplanar fragments share a layer as documented. precise.ts: the same weight as index.ts on four r32float sums (multisampleable, unlike rgba32float) and revealage plus the linear mark in rg16float by dst (1 - src), averaged per sample in the composite. meshes.ts copies position and normal (six floats a vertex) and the material at the call and restores the geometry once; vfx.ts refuses depth peeling before linking. Nothing wrong found in the five files. Open: a peel's transparent fragments behind opaque surfaces still use up layers (the opaque test runs only at the resolve), so `layers` must also cover hidden layers; with a linear layer the peel resolve may make one bind group set per peel (layers squared groups) if the snapshot view changes per call; no mutation score |

Read from the specifications, not re-fetched: PKWARE APPNOTE.TXT 4.4.21, 4.4.22 and 6.3.10 (https://pkware.cachefly.net/webdocs/casestudies/APPNOTE.TXT); W3C PNG third edition 11.3.2.1 tRNS (https://www.w3.org/TR/png-3/#11tRNS); KTX 2.0 sections 2, 3.7, 3.9.7, 3.10.3.1 and Appendices C and D.1 to D.5 (Khronos, https://registry.khronos.org/KTX/specs/2.0/ktxspec.v2.html); glTF 2.0 3.5.3, 3.6.2.4, 3.7.2.1, 3.11, 5.1, 5.11, 5.22 and 5.24. | stl.ts binary layout, the header rule (at most 80 bytes, not "solid"), face normals by the right-hand rule, the ASCII grammar; ply.ts header, binary record stride 24 (+3) and face records of 13 bytes, linear to sRGB for colours; png.ts Sub filter (mod 256), flipY through the canvas only when it redraws; shared/png.js signature, IHDR fields, chunk CRC over type and data, Paeth tie order a, b, c. Second pass: APNG acTL and the 26-byte fcTL field offsets, one sequence shared by fcTL and fdAT, cumulative millisecond delays; SHA-256 length words (bits high = floor(n / 2^29)); canonical prefix codes (bl_count offset by one, reversed for an LSB-first writer); KTX2 DFD offsets (dfdByteOffset at 48, descriptorBlockSize at +10, colorPrimaries at +13, bytesPlane at +20); camera and KHR_lights_punctual ranges; normalized accessor reads max(c / 127, -1); sparse indices ascending and -0 kept; interleaved strides 4-aligned and at most 252; Lanczos-3 kernel and premultiplied linear-light resampling; glTF-to-Unity _ST offset 1 - s - o; Unreal flipbook graph; median-cut palette. CPU 580/580 (first pass); test:browser, test:png:browser and test:capture:browser pass, test:browser and test:capture:browser also with MARGO_COMPAT=1. No GPU atomics, no plugins Fourth pass: BasisLZ Huffman table header (14-bit count, 5-bit length count, the 17, 18, 19, 20, 0, 8 order), endpoint and selector codebooks, 64-entry move-to-front history and slice predictions against Appendix D; Unreal helper API names and tuple returns; Roblox Y180 basis determinant +1, base64 and CDATA splitting; scene.ts compose, determinant3, normalMatrix, invertAffine and the winding flip; UsdSkel bind, rest and animation spaces; the Unity package's strict JSON reader and InvariantCulture parsing. | Third pass, settled from the suspect list: (1) ktx2-encode sized the external encoder's output from the pixel count (2 bytes a texel + 64 KiB), but every 4x4 block costs UASTC 16 bytes however few texels it holds, so a 16384 x 1 strip with its mips needs 131,088 bytes against 98,304 and the encode failed; (2) inWorker never saw a signal that was already aborted (an abort event fires once), so the whole encode ran, and it left its listener on a live signal for good; (3) Bin.finish() rebuilt the top-level object from a list without extras, dropping any other key (latent: no writer sets one yet); (4) packGLB wrote buffer 0 with byteLength 0 when nothing in the BIN chunk was read any more, which glTF 2.0 5.10.2 forbids (minimum 1); (5) the Godot add-on returned ERR_UNAVAILABLE, failing the whole import, for a COLOR_0 primitive with no material, which these exporters write; (6) Unity UV clips were grouped by name, so two clips with one name became one, and the clip name went into the .anim path unchecked (a/b named a sub-folder that does not exist); Roblox cels and Unreal M_ assets could share a name after clean-up, and two unnamed materials of one clip were both material0. Not a defect: KHR_gaussian_splatting kernel, colorSpace, POINTS mode, attribute names and component types match the ratified table. Third pass: encodeTriangles wrote triangle header 0xe0, but both meshopt specifications say the header must equal 0xe1, where 0xXd and 0xXe mean last - 1 and last + 1 and only vertex FIFO entries 1 to 12 are read; the stream followed the older version 0 rules (FIFO 13 and 14), so a decoder or validator that checks the header as the specifications require rejects every triangle stream the exporter wrote (decoders accepts both, so the package tests did not see it). Second pass: transformTextures read a data URI without ";base64" with decodeURIComponent, so percent-escaped image octets (a PNG starts with %89) threw URIError and characters above 0x7f were truncated to one byte. Note: a PLY or OBJ vertex colour is written alone, not times the material's base colour factor (glTF multiplies them); this is what the option documents Fourth pass: fixed (1) the Unity and Unreal precision verifiers required faces x 6 indices, so a triangle face failed; (2) their wide-index check refused 65,536 vertices on 32-bit indices and let a restart value through; (3) draco.ts cached a rejected encoder load; (4) the USDZ ZIP wrapped its entry count past 65,535 files; (5) the Unity importer's C# PNG reader ignored tRNS; (6) collision suffixes after _ or $, which Godot reads, were not read by collisionTag, the Unity profile or the Godot helper inventory; (7) exportModel never wrote mode, so points and lines read as triangles; (8) a string shininess was coerced; (9) usdz-anim wrote endTimeCode NaN for empty key times; p12c fixed the KTX2 worker jobs sharing one buffer (DataCloneError) and Unity 2D names that were not plain file names. Audit p12d to p12f then fixed (docs/known-bugs.md, "exporters audit fixes"): loaded points and lines dropped by exportModel (and unindexed strips written as lists of their vertices; glTF 2.0 3.7.2.1 and 5.24.4), flatten under shear, weldVertices index range, matrix node with weights, a repeated skin joint in usdz-anim, base colour on TEXCOORD_2, the Roblox verifier basename, Unreal rotation preflight and hook newline, roblox-chunks quadNormal, KTX2 padding without global data, the Basis and Draco build scripts, and the Unity package version and alignment checks; regressions include tests/points-lines.test.js. | Data URI octets are read per RFC 2397 (%XX is one octet, other characters their UTF-8 bytes, a bad escape is a TypeError); regression in tests/textures.test.js; encodeTriangles writes 0xe1 and codes last - 1 and last + 1 (13 and 14) as the specifications define, regression in tests/meshopt.test.js (fails on the 0xe0 encoder); (1) the output holds 20 bytes a block on every mip level plus 64 KiB; (2) inWorker refuses an aborted signal before starting a worker and removes its listener when done (both in tests/basis-native.test.js); (3) finish() keeps every key, extras in order; (4) the emptied BIN buffer goes and later buffer references move down (both in tests/glb-read.test.js, the second checked by the Khronos validator); (5) such a primitive gets glTF's default material, then marked linear (tests/godot.test.js); (6) clips group by index, and one namer makes Unity file, Roblox cel and Unreal asset names safe and distinct ignoring case, with new UnityUVClip.file and UnrealFlipbook.asset fields (tests/engine-uv.test.js). Each test fails on the code before its fix Fourth pass regressions, each failing before its fix: tests/precision-bundles.test.js (triangle strip of 1, 254 and 65,534 faces), tests/draco-load-retry.test.js, tests/usdz-zip-limit.test.js, tests/unity-png-trns.test.js (compiles the C# with Unity's Mono mcs where installed), tests/godot.test.js, tests/import-names.test.js, tests/unity.test.js, tests/back-side.test.js, tests/material-shading.test.js and tests/usdz-anim.test.js; p12c: tests/ktx2.test.js and tests/unity-2d.test.js. | Unity main-object names of saved mesh and clip assets, open in docs/known-bugs.md until a Unity batch-mode harness for the companion runs it; the engine exporters write a loaded model's points and lines only through exportModel (OBJ, PLY, COLLADA and USD forms not yet written, docs/limits.md). Then read: bruneton.ts (phases, table mapping and its inverse, the four orders and the ground bounce bookkeeping), lighting.ts and project.ts (Karis 2014 environment BRDF, the nine harmonics' constants and solid angles), fog.ts, reflection.ts, cloud-shadows.ts and shafts.ts (shell roots, Mitchell 2007 march), aerial.ts (slice mapping), hillaire.ts sky-view mapping, the weather snow coverage integrals: found the multiple-scattering table built at texel centres and read at the edge mapping, and both tables' top rows marched from a point on the top sphere, where sphereHit took the near root, about 0 (transmittance texel (255, 63): 1 against 0.0113), both fixed in 7dc64b9e4 (the tables built at i / 31, j / 31 and marched to distanceToTop, the far root; regression: the hillaire case of test/features-check.mjs, five texels of each table against a CPU build); Mars' longitude and season terms need Mallama 2007's tables, not openly available (open, known-bugs). Third pass (2026-11-14): HillaireSky and AerialPerspective put the eye a metre above the ground, which Float32 rounds away on a planet wider than about 8,000 km (60,000 km: eye radius 60000 equal to the ground, every upward ray met the ground at 0, a black sky and black air); the bake already kept 2^-22 of the radius, now all three share eyeRadius (regression: the wide planet test of test/aerial.test.js, fails before). aerial.ts sphereHit(o, d, bottom) is safe: the eye is that clearance above the ground, so the near root is taken, never the on-sphere far root. volumetric-clouds.ts: the coverage map (the shape volume's green at 0.17 of the shapes' frequency) drifted with the shapes' wind drift, wrapped at `scale`, which is 0.17 of the map's repeat, so the whole sky's cover jumped at each wrap (default wind and scale: every 23 minutes); the map now takes its own drift wrapped at scale / 0.17 (`clouds.drift[3]`, `[4]`, uniform flags.zw; regression: the coverage map test of test/volumetric-clouds.test.js, fails before). weather-fx.ts: a splash's hash key was slot x 65536 + landing in u32, which wrapped past slot 65,535, so with more than 65,536 splashes (the option allows 100,000) slots 65,536 and up landed where slots 0 to 34,463 had; now slot x 32768 + landing mod 32768 (regression: the splash key test of test/weather-fx.test.js, fails before). VolumetricClouds `maxDistance` documented as the farthest cloud drawn, but it bounds the stretch each view ray marches inside the layer from where it enters (a camera under the layer sees horizon cloud from about 138 km, faded by the haze), as Unreal's tracing max distance does; the option's text now says so. Also read line by line, no further defect: base.ts outside compose (sunDirectionFromAngles, the lighting, environment, atmosphere, layer and fog passes and their order, onPreUpdate's day and night blend, moon and sunlight, prepare's uniform layout and table refresh, recode, release); hillaire.ts (airSegment's roots, the sky-view build against its inverse lookup, the ground bounce, the disc E / (pi alpha^2) and its 65504 clamp, sunThrough's squared spacing and dt = 2 L u / n, the environment bake and its unresolved-star mean); volumetric-clouds.ts (Worley, Perlin with the quintic fade, Perlin-Worley remap, the density and height profile, cloudShell's constant and stable roots, the Henyey-Greenstein lobes and octaves, the energy-conserving step, the 64 x 32 deck and its direction against environmentDirection, the sky light reduction, the uniform layout); weather.ts (the particle box, drift wraps and the 10 pi sway phase, the swept flake's coverage, rain streaks, the lightning Poisson process, strokes and thunder delay); weather-fx.ts (the cover map's projection against rainCover's decode, splashes, lens drops and wetness). The 2D cloud layer of base.ts keeps its drift unwrapped in Float32 (its noise does not repeat), so after about 24 days at the default 8 m/s it moves in 2 m steps (documented in limits.md). Not fetched: the IAU WGCCRE reports (Saturn's pole) Former scenery entries: Moon shading is Lambert, so a full Moon darkens toward its limb where the real Moon (close to Lommel-Seeliger) stays flat; the clean-room note calls it a visual model, not a lunar BRDF. The Milky Way band, star clouds and Great Rift are a Margo shape model, not fitted to Mellinger's photometry (not read). Not read: Hipparcos Vol. 1 (ESA SP-1200), Kasten and Young 1989 (Applied Optics, paywalled), Koschmieder 1924, CIE S 011, Darula and Kittler 2002, Leinert et al. 1998, Seidelmann et al. 2007, the three aurora books. CPU 383/383, `tsc --noEmit` clean | Open, by design: `Points`, `Line` and `Segments` rebuild for a new `points`/`material` object by design (README: "Changed data/style rebuilds"), so an inline `material` object rebuilds the cloud each render. `physics.tsx` (742 lines) not read: physics is on hold. Entry sizes (texture-cache.ts is now also in the materials entry) were not re-measured here; the records job owns `entry-sizes.json`. No mutation score |
| react (second pass 2026-11-15: this pass read geometry.tsx, float.tsx, center.tsx, bounds.tsx, billboard.tsx, frame-priority.ts, hooks.ts, keyboard-controls.tsx and scroll-controls.tsx line by line, and components.tsx in full, cameras.tsx lines 1 to 140, lod.tsx lines 1 to 90 and html.tsx lines 1 to 80; components.tsx from line 330, physics.tsx, composer.tsx, ui.tsx, animation.tsx, xr.tsx and the other bindings, about 7,100 lines, not read) | None fetched this pass; the bindings wrap Margo packages and carry no algorithm of their own beyond the transforms checked here | Center's parent-space correction (adjugate of the parent's 3 by 3 over its determinant, column-major); Billboard's local rotation (parent columns without scale against the camera-facing basis) and its matrix-to-quaternion branches (core normalises); Float's bob range mapping; Bounds' clip planes around core's fit distance; frame callbacks' ordering snapshot (additions next frame, removals at once). Primitive coverage against the rivals: only Box, Sphere and Plane had components, while the rivals' JSX covers every builder | Gap: 17 of the 20 builders in `@margoengine/geometry` (cylinder, cone, capsule, circle, ring, torus, torus knot, the four Platonic solids, polyhedron, lathe, tube, shape, extrude, rounded box) had no component or geometry child | Built: a shape component and a geometry child for every builder; `args` count by value for numbers, arrays and plain objects (inline literals do not rebuild) and by identity for Shape and curve objects; react/geometry 5,656 to 6,223 B gzip. Test: tests/kit-helpers.test.js "every geometry builder has a shape component and a geometry child"; README "Every primitive" | Rows still owed: the files named as not read. Built since: CameraShake (Perlin 2002 gradient noise, Eiserloh GDC 2016 trauma squared, both used from the record and not re-read; it restores the target and up it set each frame unless something else changed them), useCursor (on the canvas, not the page), useViewport and useAspect (cover sizing at the camera target) in `./helpers`, and Stats (the readout alone, on in every build) in `./devtools` (tests/kit-helpers.test.js). PbrMaterial and MeshTransmissionMaterial rebuilt on every render for an inline colour array (fixed, KB-REACT-COLOR1, tests/react.test.js). Third pass (2026-11-15): animation.tsx, xr.tsx, ui.tsx and composer.tsx read line by line (physics.tsx not read: physics is on hold). Fixed: a non-looping blended clip at a speed other than 1 ended at the wrong clip time, the clamp ignored the Animator's speed and clip rate (tests/material-animation.test.js); SpriteLayer recreated its layer for every inline `clear` tuple and ignored new `filter`, `mipmaps`, `shader` and `mask` (tests/react.test.js); EffectComposer's first prop change dropped the quality-tier preset values SSAO, GTAO, SSR and SSGI filled in (tests/post.test.js). Built: CameraControls in ./controls (OrbitControls of `@margoengine/controls/transitions`, damping on, a ref with setLookAt, setPosition, setTarget, rotate, rotateTo, dolly, dollyTo, truck, fitToBox and fitToSphere for boxes, spheres and meshes, saveState, reset and cancel; tests/camera-controls.test.js against the real controls). Built: useIntersect in ./helpers, by the engine's frustum culling of the last drawn frame, for a mesh, a group (any mesh with geometry under it) or a loaded model (its world bounds against the camera planes), hidden ancestors counting as not drawn (tests/kit-helpers.test.js). Built: Clone in ./helpers, a copy of a mesh tree (shared geometry and material, as drei) with models under it copied through `cloneModel` of `@margoengine/animation` at their current material values (tests/kit-helpers.test.js). Built: Preload in ./helpers (one draw with culling off at mount and after loading settles with a new model; `all` draws hidden meshes), BakeShadows in ./shadows (every caster static while mounted; the still map still follows cascade and light changes) and AdaptiveEvents in ./performance (pauses hover picking only while the factor is below 1; clicks keep picking, where drei drops every event) (tests/kit-helpers.test.js, tests/events.test.js). Built: FlyControls, FirstPersonControls and TrackballControls in ./controls, each loading only its own entry of `@margoengine/controls` (a new `./first-person` entry there, so the first-person controls no longer need the package index with drag and its BVH), every live field a prop with a removed prop restoring the default, and `keyTarget` (tests/camera-controls.test.js against the real controls). Built: DragControls in ./controls, its children dragged as one group (transformGroup of `@margoengine/controls/drag`) on the view plane, a plane or an axis lock, or turned, with per-axis position limits applied after each move and drei's start, drag, end and hover callbacks (tests/kit-helpers.test.js). Built: Wireframe in ./effects-extra, every welded triangle edge once from the new `wireframeEdges` of `@margoengine/lines/edges` (`simplify` uses featureEdges at 0 degrees to drop flat diagonals), with every line option; the mesh's material is the fill (tests/effects-extra.test.js, lines tests/edges.test.js). Built: PivotControls in ./controls on the new `all` mode of `@margoengine/controls/transform` (move arrows, plane sliders, centre, rings `RX`/`RY`/`RZ` and scale cubes `SX`/`SY`/`SZ`, each handle its own name, and a `hide` list), with drei's matrix, autoTransform, activeAxes, disable flags, translation, rotation and scale limits, depthTest, snaps and drag callbacks with local, world and delta matrices (tests/pivot-controls.test.js against the real controls on a real core engine; controls tests/transform-all.test.js); still short of drei there: `anchor` and `offset`, `axisColors` and `hoveredColor`, `lineWidth`, the drag value `annotations`, and a world-sized gizmo (`fixed` false; Margo's keeps a constant screen size). Fixed: TransformControls kept its first `pivot`, `flipAxes`, `showDelta`, `occlude` and `occluder` after a rerender and rejected `mode="all"` (tests/pivot-controls.test.js). Built: Text3D in ./text, extruded text from a TTF or OTF font (drei needs a typeface JSON) through `extrudeText` of `@margoengine/text/extrude` with every option and drei's `height` alias, a URL font fetched once and shared, string children as the text and other children inside the mesh (tests/text3d.test.js on the real extruder and a real font); Built since: drei's `smooth` (normals averaged around each position within that distance, through `creasedNormals` of `@margoengine/geometry/utils`) plus `smoothAngle`, a crease angle drei lacks (tests/text3d.test.js). Built: Svg in ./geometry, each path's fill (`toShapes` of `@margoengine/loaders/svg` through `shapeGeometry`) and stroke (`toStrokeGeometry`) one unlit transparent mesh with the SVG colour and opacity, painted in document order through a new `renderOrder` on TextureMaterial, with drei's `src` (URL or markup), `skipFill`, `skipStrokes`, `fillMaterial`, `strokeMaterial`, `fillMeshProps` and `strokeMeshProps`, plus `flipY` (tests/svg.test.js on the real loader, builders and materials). Built: ScreenQuad in ./effects-extra, one never-culled triangle over the clip square (no diagonal seam) as a RawMesh of `@margoengine/shader/raw` in a RawShaders plugin shared per scene, drawn by a GLSL `ShaderMaterial` built from `fragmentShader` and `uniforms` (values copied each render) or a supplied material (tests/screen-quad.test.js on the real shader package). Svg, ScreenQuad and Text3D `smooth` also run on a real GPU, natively and on the WebGL2 compatibility layer (`npm run test:browser:shapes --workspace @margoengine/react`, with and without MARGO_COMPAT=1, Apple M6, 2026-11-16): an Svg fill and an unfilled stroked square land in their colours with the inside showing the background, ScreenQuad's `vUv` reaches each screen corner with y up, and a smoothed bevelled Text3D keeps the flat one's silhouette with different shading. Svg failed on the small WebGL2 preview (`backend: 'webgl2'`), whose renderer has no materials draw queue; there it now draws each part as a lit, opaque core mesh a small +z step apart in document order (fixed; the same browser test runs Svg on the preview). Gaps against the rivals still open: Clouds, Outlines (Margo has Outline), SoftShadows, Lightformer, MeshReflectorMaterial, MeshPortalMaterial. Built 2026-11-16: Caustics in ./caustics (its own entry, moved out of ./geometry the same day so Box and Sphere users do not load it: geometry 7,964 B gzip measured again, caustics 3,173 B), drei's Caustics as the photon-traced catcher of `@margoengine/studio/caustics` (GPU BVH of the children's world triangles, refraction in and out with `ior`, `backsideIOR`, `backside` and `maxEvents`, a floor map under them; `floor` defaulting to the group's world position, `size`, `height`, `lightDirection` or `lightPosition`, `photonSide`, `resolution`, `frames`, `worldRadius`, `intensity`, `color`), re-traced when a child moves or changes and restarted when an option changes, `onReady` handing out the native catcher (tests/caustics.test.js with the catcher recorded: world-space triangles, one re-trace per move, live options, release; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, scripts/test-stage-parts-browser.mjs reads the map under a glass ball: finite, peak about 290 times the mean at the map centre). Built 2026-11-16: MotionPathControls and useMotion in ./controls-extra, drei's: the Canvas camera or a mesh moved along any 3D curves of the geometry package joined by arc length, looking along the path or at a damped `focus` (point or mesh), `offset` eased by a frame-rate-independent exponential with time constant `damping`, `loop` easing the short way round, and a live `{ current, point, tangent, focus }` state written by `useMotion()` or the ref (tests/motion-path.test.js on real LineCurve3 paths: arc-length positions across segments, the easing after one time constant, the seam, a mesh turning +Z to its focus; CPU only, no GPU path of its own). Built 2026-11-16: MarchingCubes, MarchingCube and MarchingPlane in ./marching-cubes (its own entry, moved out of ./geometry the same day, 4,011 B gzip), drei's metaballs over `marchingCubes` of `@margoengine/procedural` (a new optional peer) from a sampled grid of isolation minus the metaball field (each ball strength / d^2 - subtract where positive, d in units of the -1..1 cube's width, written only into the grid points it reaches; planes the same of the distance to a low face), `resolution` 28, `isolation` 80, ball `strength` 0.5 and `subtract` 12 by default, vertex colours blended by field weight for a TextureMaterial child, live ball and plane handles and a rebuild only on frames where a value changed (tests/marching-cubes.test.js on the real procedural extraction: sphere radius 2 sqrt(0.5/92) with outward normals, a moved ref rebuilding, colours by weight, a floor at the plane distance; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, scripts/test-stage-parts-browser.mjs draws a red ball through its vertex colours). Extended 2026-11-16: `maxPolyCount` (default 10000; the triangles past it are dropped and the kept ones' vertices compacted), `enableUvs` and `enableColors` (default false), the defaults of three.js's MarchingCubes constructor, which drei passes through (three.js docs, https://threejs.org/docs/pages/MarchingCubes.html, read: `new MarchingCubes(resolution, material, enableUvs = false, enableColors = false, maxPolyCount = 10000)`); vertex colours were written whenever a ball had a colour before and now need `enableColors` (README, limits); the entry grew 4,011 to 4,487 B gzip (tests/marching-cubes.test.js "uvs and colours only when enabled"). The procedural extractor under it is now axis-edge Marching Cubes, so a lone default ball at resolution 28 is at most 7.3% over its radius (was 24%). Sources checked 2026-11-16: W. E. Lorensen and H. E. Cline, "Marching Cubes: A High Resolution 3D Surface Construction Algorithm" (SIGGRAPH 1987, https://doi.org/10.1145/37402.37422), read: edge crossings by linear interpolation and the normal as the field gradient (eq. 1), estimated there by central differences (eqs. 2 to 4) and interpolated to the vertex, which `marchingCubes` of procedural does; G. Wyvill, C. McPheeters and B. Wyvill, "Data structure for soft objects" (The Visual Computer 2(4), 1986, https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01900346), read: summed key-point fields, an iso value ("magic"), the same linear edge interpolation, and a falloff C(r) with C(R) = 0 and C'(R) = 0 (the r^6, r^4, r^2 polynomial, a = -0.444444, b = 1.888889, c = -2.444444); drei's MarchingCubes docs page (https://drei.docs.pmnd.rs/abstractions/marching-cubes), read: the props `resolution`, `maxPolyCount`, `enableUvs`, `enableColors`, ball `strength`, `subtract`, `color`, `position` and plane `planeType`, with no stated semantics or defaults. J. F. Blinn, "A Generalization of Algebraic Surface Drawing" (ACM TOG 1(3), 1982, https://doi.org/10.1145/357306.357310), not read: every copy found refused the download; only Wyvill et al.'s account of it (an exponential electron-density field) was used. Findings: the falloff strength / d^2 - subtract is drei's, not Blinn's Gaussian or Wyvill's polynomial, and the code comment that credited it to them is corrected; it is kept for drei parity, with the slope kink at d = sqrt(strength / subtract) that Wyvill's C'(R) = 0 avoids recorded in docs/limits.md. Normals were the grid's central differences, up to 32 degrees off the true normal at the default resolution, where a default ball spans about two cells of a near-singular 1/d^2 field; they are now the field's exact gradient at each vertex (fixed, tests/marching-cubes.test.js "normals are the field gradient", failing before at 15 degrees). Positions keep the linear edge interpolation of both papers: a lone default ball at resolution 28 comes out up to about 24% larger in radius than the exact sphere, about 8% at 48 (docs/limits.md). `maxPolyCount`, `enableUvs` and `enableColors` are not taken: there is no polygon cap, uvs are always written, and colours are written when a ball has one. Built 2026-11-16: Sampler and useSurfaceSampler in ./sampler (its own entry, moved out of ./instances the same day: instances back to 3,421 B gzip, sampler 4,127 B), drei's: `count` points on a mesh surface (a ref, a native mesh or a core geometry, followed each frame so a lazily built or replaced shape geometry is picked up) by `MeshSurfaceSampler` of `@margoengine/geometry/sample` (area or per-vertex `weights`, `colors`, `seed`), drawn as one Instances draw with a `transform(point)` that gets position, normal, uv, colour and the Euler XYZ rotation standing +Y on the normal (tests/sampler.test.js against the geometry sampler and the instance world rotation; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, scripts/test-stage-parts-browser.mjs draws 1000 sampled boxes over a square). Built 2026-11-16: useDepthBuffer in ./portals, drei's useDepthBuffer: the scene's depth from the Canvas camera drawn by a target view before the main view into an owned `size` x `size` depth texture of useFBO (scene depth format and sample count), for the first `frames` frames of each target (default every frame) and kept afterwards, the main view held while it is the only canvas view (tests/depth-buffer.test.js on a recording device; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, scripts/test-stage-parts-browser.mjs loads the texture after its 3 frames: a plane's depth at the centre, the clear value at the corner). Built 2026-11-16: Mask and useMask in ./materials, drei's Mask: the mask mesh writes its `id` (1..255) into the stencil buffer with colour and depth writes off by default and draws first (render order -1); `useMask(id, inverse)` returns the stencil options (`equal`, or `not-equal` inverted, write mask 0) to spread into a `<TextureMaterial>`; the materials package's portable `StencilBuffer` is attached once per scene while a Mask is mounted (tests/mask.test.js on the real materials package; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, scripts/test-stage-parts-browser.mjs reads red inside a mask and black outside, and the reverse inverted). Built 2026-11-16: Segments in ./lines, drei's Segments and Segment: every Segment is one pair of one native line in segment mode (one draw), `limit` in mount order, `color` per segment multiplying the line colour, every `<Line>` material and transform prop, and a live `{ start, end, color }` ref written in place that uploads only on frames where a value changed (drei rewrites its buffer every frame) (tests/segments.test.js on the real lines Line). Built 2026-11-16: Backdrop in ./geometry, drei's Backdrop as the staging package's cyclorama (`backdropGeometry` of `@margoengine/studio/backdrop`, a new optional peer: floor bending into a wall with exact analytic normals, `width`, `depth`, `height`, `radius`, `segments`), receiving shadows, a matte default replaced by any material child, and UVs across the width and along the sweep by arc length so texture materials fit it (tests/backdrop.test.js on the real staging builder). Built 2026-11-16: useFBO in ./portals, drei's useFBO as an owned view target (canvas-sized and following resizes unless sized, `format`, `depth` in the scene's depth format and samples, recreated on device restore, freed on unmount; tests/fbo.test.js on a recording device; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, a fixed, a canvas-sized and a depth-carrying target each match core views drawing the same scene into a hand-made texture pixel for pixel, `npm run test:browser:fbo --workspace @margoengine/react`). Built 2026-11-16: Merged in ./instances, drei's Merged over `<Instances>`: one instanced draw per source mesh, components per source (array as arguments, record as a record), `limit` and `range` per source, every `<Instance>` prop per copy (tests/merged.test.js). KeyboardControls (fixed, KB-REACT-KEYS1): a `key` entry such as 'w' let go while W was held once Shift went down, never matched Shift+W, and with an empty `code` stayed down after release; one-character keys now match in either case, since W3C UI Events KeyboardEvent gives `key` with the modifier state applied and `code` as the physical key (from the record, not re-fetched) (regression in tests/controls-extra.test.js). Digits and symbols with no `code` still change with Shift by layout; the README names them by code PivotControls gained drei's `anchor` (the children's box in the group's own space, followed as children load), `offset`, `fixed`, `axisColors`, `hoveredColor`, `lineWidth`, `annotations` and `annotationsClass` (distance, degrees or scale factor at the gizmo during a drag, through a lazily loaded `<Html>`), and TransformControls passes the new gizmo options through on every rerender (tests/pivot-controls.test.js). PivotControls `fixed` now defaults to false as drei's (the gizmo `scale` world units long; true keeps a constant on-screen size), so code moved from drei keeps its gizmo size (tests/pivot-controls.test.js).Built 2026-11-16: CubeCamera in ./environment, drei's: the scene around the group's world origin rendered into a cube texture by `CubeCamera` of `@margoengine/environment/cube-camera` for the first `frames` frames (default every frame), the render-function children drawn with that texture and left out of the capture (every mesh under the group), `resolution`, `near`, `far`, `mips` and `layers`, the texture handed again when a device restore replaces it; PbrMaterial and MappedPbrMaterial gained `envMap` (six-layer GPUTexture, cube Texture, Environment or cube target) and `envMapIntensity` (tests/cube-camera.test.js, the envMap case failed first; on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, `npm run test:browser:reflections --workspace @margoengine/react` shows a mirror ball reflecting a red wall behind the canvas camera, and the same ball without envMap does not). Sizes: react/environment 2,875 to 4,893 B gzip, mostly the shared root Mesh chunk. Built 2026-11-16: CubeCamera `envMap` and `fog`, drei's: an .hdr or .exr drawn as the faces' background in place of the scene's (the native CubeCamera gained `background`, which the capture draws at the first scene background pass's order, or 0.5, while it skips the passes EnvironmentBackground, ImageBackground and ColorBackground mark as background; faces wait for the load so `frames={1}` cannot miss it) and fog options or false applied to the scene's Fog for the faces and restored after (with no scene Fog, a private one, off in the main view and added to the Canvas's glTF PBR models). Evidence: packages/environment/tests/mirror.test.js (the background case failed first), packages/react/tests/cube-camera-capture.test.js, and on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, `npm run test:browser:reflections --workspace @margoengine/react` modes cube-camera-fog (private green fog reaches the reflected wall), cube-camera-fog-off (the scene fog is off in the faces while the main view keeps it), cube-camera-scene-fog (baseline) and cube-camera-env-map (an HDR sky in the reflection, the main view still dark). Size check: an app importing only `Environment` from ./environment bundles 2,304 B gzip (sideEffects false drops CubeCamera and its Mesh); Canvas plus Environment 9,099 B against Canvas alone 7,451 B, so no split was needed. Sizes for envMap and fog: react/environment 4,893 to 5,507 B gzip (the fog swap, the private fog and the capture environment's load, which only CubeCamera users bundle), environment/cube-camera 5,799 to 5,925 B and reflector and refractor +102 and +103 B (the shared capture graph's background swap). MeshReflectorMaterial (2026-11-16, drei's): gap: no planar reflection over a lit mesh. Built: `MeshReflector` in environment/reflector-pbr (a Reflector variant blended over the mesh: Schlick weight of F0 raised by `mirror`, times `mixStrength`; box blur from the capture mips with `mixBlur`; depth fade and blur from the captured depth's distance to the plane; contrast; `reflectorOffset`; normal-map distortion; PlanarSurface gained a protected `surfaceBlend` that turns off depth writes and biases the draw) and `<MeshReflectorMaterial>` in react/materials (the mesh keeps its PbrMaterial; the reflector is parented to the mesh, fitted to its flat geometry and leaves it out of the capture; `resolution` in pixels, `blur` as one number or two, `mixBlur` times `roughness`, F0 from colour and metalness after glTF 2.0 Appendix B). Sources: Schlick 1994 (Computer Graphics Forum 13(3), from the record, not re-read), drei's documented props only (no code read). Tests: environment tests/mesh-reflector.test.js and tests/mesh-reflector-browser.js (Schlick blend exact to 0.0005 on rgba16float, coplanar draw, mixStrength 0 unchanged, blur, depth fade, offset; native and MARGO_COMPAT=1), react tests/mesh-reflector-material.test.js and the mesh-reflector modes of tests/reflections-browser.tsx (native and MARGO_COMPAT=1). The depth bias passed the coplanar check on Metal with or without it (kept as a guard, not a fix). Sizes: react/materials 7,366 to 8,493 B, environment/reflector-pbr 3,300 to 4,910 B. Differences from drei in limits.md (Mesh reflector). Built 2026-11-16: `<Environment>` children (resolution, frames, near, far; captured by a native CubeCamera into an Environment over the src environment or black, hidden in the main view) and `<Lightformer>` (form rect, circle, ring, box; intensity, color, scale, target) on the new environment/lightformer entry, an unlit emissive pass. Sources: Debevec 1998 (SIGGRAPH, image-based lighting, from the record, not re-read), drei's documented Lightformer and Environment props only (no code read); open: Pellacini 2010 envyLight (abstract only, not cited). Tests: react tests/lightformer.test.js (fails without the change: no `Lightformer` export, children ignored), environment tests/lightformer-browser.js and the lightformer modes of react tests/reflections-browser.tsx (native and MARGO_COMPAT=1). Sizes: environment/lightformer new 4,300 B, react/environment 5,763 to 7,105 B. Differences in limits.md (Lightformer and Environment children). Built 2026-11-16: AccumulativeShadows and RandomizedLight in ./accumulative-shadows (its own entry, studio peer), drei's: `AmbientShadows` of `@margoengine/studio/accumulate-ambient` under the group (world position and up), every `castShadow` mesh and model of the Canvas added as a caster by the binding (a new per-engine caster set that Mesh and Model fill whether or not `<Shadows>` is on; a `<TextureMaterial>` mesh casts through its materials mesh, which the first GPU run caught: its core geometry is cleared), RandomizedLight disks placed 1000 times their distance along their direction for drei's directional lights, `ambient` as the intensity-weighted mean, `frames` and `blend` in frames of the summed `amount`, `frames={Infinity}` with `blend` and `limit`, `temporal`, `scale`, `opacity`, `color`, `resolution`, and drei's ref `getMesh`, `reset` and `update(frames)`; drei's `alphaTest` (a caster cut-out), `colorBlend` and `toneMapped` (PBR Neutral) and RandomizedLight's shadow camera (`size` window, `mapSize` grid, `near` and `far` planes from the light's centre, `bias`), and the `frames` prop, which an early build dropped so every catcher took 40 frames (docs/limits.md). The plane colour is sRGB encoded as core encodes a surface (it was written linear, too dark for any colour other than black). Tests: tests/accumulative-shadows.test.js (mapping, casters following castShadow, live props, ref, lights, invalid input, materials meshes); on a real GPU, natively and with MARGO_COMPAT=1, scripts/test-stage-parts-browser.mjs mode accumulative-shadows draws a white shadow 1 unit beside a PbrMaterial box and a TextureMaterial box lit at 45 degrees and leaves the mirrored spots clear. That check caught a studio bug: disk samples took the near plane from their own point, so a light 1000 times out with its radius scaled alike clipped every caster for the half of its disk nearer the plane; near and far now stand at the light's centre distance (studio test/accumulate-ambient.test.js). Sources: drei's AccumulativeShadows and RandomizedLight docs pages (https://drei.docs.pmnd.rs/staging/accumulative-shadows, https://drei.docs.pmnd.rs/staging/randomized-light), read for props and defaults; no code read. Built 2026-11-16: Clouds and Cloud in ./clouds (its own entry, 3,247 B gzip), drei's puff clouds over the new `@margoengine/vfx/clouds` (8,006 B): every Cloud in a Clouds is one instanced draw sorted far to near on the CPU, with `limit`, `range`, `texture` (a URL fetched before anything draws), `frustumCulled`, `material` as Margo's shading ('scatter' or 'unlit'), `seed`, `segments`, `bounds`, `concentrate`, `volume`, `smallestVolume`, `distribute`, `growth`, `speed`, `fade`, `opacity` and `color`, plus light through the cloud (Harris, GDC 2002, read in full), `softness` against the scene depth with Lorach's contrast curve (NVIDIA 2007, read in full) and fog; Harris and Lastra 2001 and Wang 2004 abstracts only; drei read from its docs page only (tests/clouds.test.js, packages/vfx/test/clouds.test.js and clouds-check.mjs: one draw, soft edges, near fade and identical bytes for the same deltas, native and WebGL2 compatibility). CPU 272/272 (1 test extended). smooth-lod.ts and smooth-clip.ts written this pass: nodes chosen and handed over as lod.ts; each node clipped to its box with cut points keyed by their edge, every face closed by a cap (the solid part of its plane; replaced 4-cell skirts, which let 169 of 32,009 rays through a near-parallel seam; KB-VOX-CAP1, fine 3D noise, fixed 2026-11-16 by counting caps by winding number with both faces at a box edge starting from the same numbers); 6,000 rays from the air into the ground through real chunks and three levels: 0 pass with caps, 37 without; node meshes closed (every edge used twice); level points within one cell of the true ground; GPU check native and WebGL2 compatibility: 0 sky pixels through the ground with caps, 11 without | vox.ts: a dictionary string that is not UTF-8 (a name saved in a Windows code page) was read as windows-1252 but written back as UTF-8, so `writeVox(parseVox(file))` changed the file's bytes although the README promised it byte for byte; a code comment admitted it. Two other code-comment notes (sortQuads centre order, the 1/128 pixel T-junction margin) were limits missing from docs/limits.md. sortQuads ordered translucent quads by centre distance, so a merged water top drew over glass in front of it, and docs/limits.md sent users to `@margoengine/oit`, which voxel cannot use (it has no OIT hook); smooth.ts: past an unloaded chunk every axis repeated the border, so where a chunk was missing beside two loaded neighbours they computed their shared corner points from different samples and the surface did not meet there chunk.ts and world.ts read line by line (2026-11-15): a palette widened to more than 16 slots while some slots were free (VoxelChunk grow, reached by pasteVoxModel's one-step reserve) rebuilt the state lookup from every slot, freed ones included, so a pasted state that matched a freed slot's old value reused that slot while it stayed on the free list and the next new state overwrote it: the first pasted cell read as a later state. fluids.ts read line by line: a reach of 4, 5 or 6 stepped one level a cell (floor(7 / r) = 1) and dried up only past level 7, so it ran 7 cells where the option promises r; update() after dispose() threw only once a whole tick had built up, not as documented. textures.ts: BlockTextures.load closed no bitmap when one URL failed (Promise.all rejected first, the others' ImageBitmaps leaked), and fromAtlas's error said an atlas needs 1 to 256 tiles where the array takes 2,048. blocks.ts connectStairs: a stair at the end of a straight run turned into a corner toward a perpendicular stair behind or in front, cutting a notch into the run's back wall (run facing +x at z 4 and 5, a +z stair behind (5, 5): (5, 5) became outer+z). objects.ts update: when a placement threw part way through a newly loaded chunk, the placements made before it were left out of the stored cell map: dispose never released them and the retry placed them again (two cells placed, the third failing: 4 meshes leaked per try). define accepted a source graph 64 meshes deep that no placement could hold. wind.ts bindings: the base phase used the raw direction where the shader uses the unit one, so a wind of length other than 1 on a rebased renderer was shifted in phase and jumped whenever the base moved (direction [0.3, 0], base x 1024: 2.436 rad uploaded, 4.365 owed). debris.ts read in full with no defect. bevel.ts read in full: chamfer offsets come from merged quad corners, so a T-junction cracks and coplanar quads groove (open in known-bugs.md, beveled voxel chamfers crack). gpu-mesher.ts #run: a job whose CPU path threw (meshInput, the block flags, meshChunk, sortQuads) had already left the queue and was outside the GPU batch, so its mesh promise never settled and the renderer waited on that chunk forever (a slab type whose layer is not one of the four: pending after 1 s). smooth.ts read in full with no defect. light-rgb.ts: strength was never checked, so NaN, a negative, Infinity or a string reached the window uniform and the shader coloured every lit pixel NaN or negative; the rest (channel copies, setColor, the wrapping window, the texel reorder) read with no defect. gi.ts CPU side: after a device loss the restored device never got new volumes (update kept writing the lost device's buffers and the renderer bound its textures), and a live strength outside 0 to 8 went to the shader unchecked. bevel.ts: chamfers cracked at merged-quad T-junctions and grooved coplanar quads (offsets came from quad corners). smooth.ts: SmoothTerrainView.update dropped a chunk from dirty and its old meshes before a throwing createMesh, leaving it half drawn. Fourth pass 2026-11-16: the rest of render.ts and vox.ts read line by line. render.ts voxelHit: a fragment's plane hit mapped its pixel to NDC over the whole target, but a fragment position counts from the target's corner and a view covers only its rectangle, so in any view not at the full target (split screen, picture in picture) textures, AO and light were shaded from another pixel's ray (two side-by-side views of one camera: 23,209 of 38,400 pixels differed between the halves). | parseVox records per dictionary which keys and values were windows-1252 and writeVox writes them back in windows-1252 while unchanged (a value set since goes out as UTF-8); regression in tests/vox.test.js (fails on the old code: 0xc3a9 written for 0xe9). VoxelObjects.update stores the chunk cell map in a finally, so made placements stay owned and the chunk stays pending; define refuses graphs 64 or more deep; regressions tests/objects.test.js, limits.md object blocks. VoxelWind folds the base in with the float32 unit direction the shader divides out; regression tests/wind.test.js. GpuVoxelMesher rejects that job with the error and goes on; regression tests/gpu-mesher.test.js. VoxelColorLight checks strength at attach and at each update; regression tests/light-rgb.test.js, README errors bullet. VoxelGI remakes its GPU state on the engine's new device (old one freed, window traced again) and checks strength each update; regressions tests/gi.test.js, README device restore and errors bullets. bevel.ts redesigned around cell silhouettes: unit-edge crease classification, inset panels, half strips, corner polygons (face, edge and vertex pieces of Doo and Sabin 1978, CAD 10(6), not read in full) and T-junction insertion (Lysenko 2012, 0fps.net greedy meshing article, not read in full); closed for every merge; context faces close chunk seams; tests/bevel-silhouette.test.js. Renderer seams (2026-11-16): the meshers return the shell's whole faces that touch a chunk (`ChunkMesh.border`, border.ts, CPU and GPU meshers alike) and a beveled renderer passes them as context; on a seam every corner-walk point goes into the edges through it, so chunks that merge their seam rows differently share their seam vertices (tests/bevel-seam.test.js, bevel-silhouette.test.js, seam pixels in bevel-browser-check and borders in gpu-mesher-browser-check, native and WebGL2 compatibility). smooth.ts: chunk meshes built first and swapped after; a throw leaves the chunk pending with its old meshes (tests/smooth.test.js). Ticks put back the updates after a throwing hook in a finally (tests/ticks.test.js); VoxelModelRenderer keeps the meshes made before a throw and the chunk pending (tests/models.test.js); known-bugs.md entries. The frame uniform carries the view's rectangle (size, then corner in f[56..57]) and voxelHit takes the corner off before mapping a pixel to the view's NDC; regression `sideBySide` in tests/render-browser-check (23,209 of 38,400 pixels differed before, 0 after, native and WebGL2 compatibility), known-bugs.md entry, README multi-view note. | Thorne 2005 (floating origin) read as its abstract only, Ring and Cozzi 2011 chapter 5 not read (Ohlarik 2008 and Persson 2012 read, docs/clean-room.md); Newell, Newell and Sancha 1972 read in summary only; Lysenko 2013 (0fps.net), Amanatides and Woo 1987, Ramamoorthi and Hanrahan 2001 and CIE 15:2004 not re-read in full this pass (rules checked from the record); MagicaVoxel's centring of rotated odd-sized models is not stated by the format notes (Margo's floor rule is checked against its own oracle only). The light-level, AO-to-light and liquid rules beyond those formulas are Margo design choices (clean-room VX3 to VX13) with no measurement on record. (3) `GLTFWeather` snow lifted a VRM body but not its MToon outline hull, and a shader without gltf/pbr's standard projection lost the lift with no error. (4) A listener's `transitionTo` was refused only after it had replaced the running transition, so the weather went to the listener's target. (5) `schedule` checked a step's ease only on reaching it: a bad later ease threw from `update`, a bad first one left a schedule with no transition. (6) `randomWalk` stopped a running schedule before refusing a bad seed. (3) The MToon outline (rigging, a minimal change) keeps the standard projection and builds its hull on `o.clip` and `o.position` after it; `GLTFWeather` throws `MargoError` `'unsupported'` when the projection is absent (regressions: rigging tests/mtoon-shader.test.js, tests/gltf.test.js; known-bugs). (4) to (6) `transitionTo`, `schedule`, `randomWalk` and `update` refuse a call from a listener, and every bad step ease or seed, before changing anything (tests/weather.test.js, three tests). Raskar and Cohen, "Image Precision Silhouette Edges" (I3D 1999), and Praun, Hoppe, Webb and Finkelstein, "Real-Time Hatching" (SIGGRAPH 2001), from the record; the circle-in-square area worked here from first principles (a segment of a disc of radius r cut by a chord at distance h is r^2 acos(h / r) - h sqrt(r^2 - h^2)) | Inverted hull: the normal through the cofactor matrix (cross products of the model columns) times the sign of the determinant, so mirrored instances push outward, normalised before the world-space offset; front culling and no depth write. Position rings: equal positions bit for bit, -0 as 0, each ring closed. Hatching layers drawn below (n - k) / (n + 1) at 180 / n degree turns; stripe coverage 1 - (1 - w / s)^layers. TrueType half (2026-11-13), noted, not changed: point matching cannot reference phantom points (the glyf chapter allows them; such a font fails with an attachment error); ROUND_XY_TO_GRID rounds in font units (grid fitting has no meaning for unhinted outlines); a platform 3 encoding 0 (Symbol) cmap is accepted as Unicode 2026-11-14, noted, not changed: extrudeText does not validate align (an unknown value lays out left); Text.set assigns before validate, so a refused set leaves the bad value in place and the next frame throws again, the same as assigning the field directly |

W3C WebGPU timestamp queries (section 20.4, implementation-defined values and counter resets) from the record | Frame beacon histogram: 256 logarithmic bins over 0.1 ms to 10 s give a bin ratio of 1.04600 (e^(ln 1e5 / 256)), matching the stated 4.6% bound, percentiles by rank ceil(p n) and the upper bin edge capped by the true maximum; WebGPU readback: start/end pairs as two 32-bit words with borrow, overlap of passes on Apple GPUs, backwards time rejected before a sample replaces the last; WebGL2: disjoint read before availability is safe because results only become available between tasks, so both checks see the same state in one poll; triangle counts per topology (list floor(n/3), strip and fan n - 2) as the README states. Second pass: the rolling frame mean (a ring total updated by the replaced sample), fps = 1000 / mean, the graph read oldest first from sampleIndex once full; the pass wrappers keep the native receiver and count draw, drawIndexed, both indirect draws and both dispatches; GPU sampling every 15th encoder; FramePhases splits update, tick (store.tick), cull, encode (createCommandEncoder or the first pass), submit and other, closed by a microtask after the frame task, with a mid-frame submit returning to the phase it left; PipelineCompiles' hitch window from the start of the frame before to the end of this one and its blocking-first order; textureBytes per format (1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 byte texels; 8-byte blocks for bc1, bc4, etc2-rgb8 and etc2-rgb8a1, eac-r11; 16 for the other bc, etc2-rgba8, eac-rg11 and every astc block) over every mip, layer and sample; statsReport's oldest-first index; LeakWatch's ring shared by every series (a series added late is judged only after a full window) and its four strictly rising quarter minima; StatsPanels sampling after FramePhases closes the frame; PassPrototypes' native-only checks before it swaps a pass's prototype. CPU 117/117 | Second pass: GPUMemory.dispose wrote the original createBuffer and createTexture back even when another GPUMemory had wrapped them since, so the later tracker (a user's beside StatsPanels' own, for one) silently stopped counting | Second pass: dispose restores only while its wrappers are outermost and otherwise passes through without counting (regression packages/stats/test/report.test.js, fails on the old source); README says trackers stack | A frame longer than 10 s lands in the last bin, so a percentile it decides reads 10,000 ms though the frame was longer (tested as designed in test/beacon.test.js; the bound stated is "at most 4.6% above", which still holds). Strip triangle counts ignore primitive restart, as the README says; second pass: no browser run (the fix is CPU-side and covered by the node test), no mutation score |
| selection (second pass 2026-11-15: every source file now read; this pass read picking.ts, picking-primitives.ts, spatial.ts, models.ts, highlight.ts and xray.ts line by line after tools.ts, area.ts and hit-details.ts in the first) | Sigg, Weyrich, Botsch and Gross, "GPU-Based Ray-Casting of Quadratic Surfaces" (SPBG 2006) fetched from the author site, but its PDF text could not be extracted here; the sphere bound was derived from the plane tangency condition (n . c + d)^2 = r^2 |n|^2 and checked against a dense surface sample. Moller and Trumbore 1997 and the even-odd crossing rule from the record | Marquee and lasso tests of projected bounds against 320,000 sampled sphere surface points; area picking's even-odd scanline at pixel centres (half-open spans ceil(edge - 0.5)); the GPU normal path (cofactor columns cross(b, c), cross(c, a), cross(a, b) times sign(det), the inverse transpose without dividing by det); unprojection of the picked depth through the inverse view-projection; CPU tests 101/101; GPU browser checks pass (picking, pickMany, models, x-ray, hit details). Second pass: the 3 x 3 micro pass crops the view-projection to the picked pixel (rows x and y become (row - n w) times rect / 3 with n the pixel centre in NDC); the frustum test pulls each Gribb and Hartmann plane (w + x, w - x, w + y, w - y, z and w - z for reversed Z in [0, 1]) into the mesh's local space and tests the local bounding sphere exactly, so nonuniform scale and mirrors stay conservative; the proxy sphere is a UV sphere whose planar quads are scaled by 1 / (least face plane distance), so the convex hull holds the unit sphere; model, proxy and instance IDs (capacity + 1 + first slot + index, then proxies, then 1-based instance hits) decode back to the same slots, including models with no primitives; front facing follows the sign of det(M) for mirrored nodes in all three shaders; core and primitive IDs pass through f32 lanes but stay at or below 2^24 + 1 because store capacity is capped at 2^23 and every model primitive and proxy anchor holds a slot, while instance hits go through u32 words; Highlight's separable Gaussian (sigma = radius / 3, taps outside the view count as zero) and the composite glow over fill a = f + g - f g with colour (fill f (1 - g) + glow g) / a for straight alpha blending; SpatialPicking's sphere query transforms each primitive and instance to world space in float64 before the BVH closest point | selectInRect and selectInPolygon sized each mesh's bounding sphere by the image of one radius offset beside its centre. Under perspective the image of a sphere is an ellipse stretched away from the view axis: off axis at a 60 degree field of view it reached 1.5 to 2.4 times that circle, so 'overlap' missed meshes whose bounds met the shape and 'contain' accepted meshes whose bounds crossed it. Second pass: SpatialPicking.ray read its hit through the mouse pick's 3 x 3 tile, so with nothing on the centre pixel it answered with a neighbour pixel's hit, up to one pixel off the ray (at 1000 m and a 60 degree field of view on a 1,000 pixel canvas, about 1 m to the side) | The screen box of the sphere is solved exactly from the tangent clip planes (a quadratic per axis); a sphere that reaches the camera plane is unbounded. 'contain' and 'overlap' test that box for marquees, and the lasso uses the circle around the centre that holds it. Regression test in test/tools.test.js (fails without the fix). A pick whose view carries the exactPick key (Symbol.for('margoengine.selection.exactPick')) reads only the centre pixel, and every spatial ray sets it; mouse picks keep their one pixel tolerance. Regression tests in test/picking.test.js and test/spatial.test.js (both fail without the fix) | The lasso's 'overlap' and 'contain' stay conservative by a circle around the projected centre, not the ellipse. Kit dependents margoengine 3/3, game, web and viz 1/1 each; stage, react, xr and animation do not call these functions and were not rerun |
