Fuzz: 2,160 mutated, truncated and size-inflated inputs over netcdf, hdf5, fits, vtk, zarr, nrrd, xdmf and ensight at 2 s each: every failure a typed error except the cases below; CPU tests | (1) diverging compared Lab hues without wrapping, so endpoints 43 degrees apart across +-180 went through white; (2) isosurface normals at the grid's faces used an unscaled one-sided difference, tilting them up to 11 degrees (CPU Wasm and WGSL); (3) an HDF5 enumeration took byte order and sign from its member count; (4) FITS rejected HISTORY and COMMENT cards with '=' in column 9 and complex values, and read a blank value as 0 (BSCALE 0 zeroed the image); (5) a FITS table of zero-width rows looped to out-of-memory; (6) an Xdmf polyhedron count and a CoRectMesh Dimensions attribute looped or allocated without bound; (7) an EnSight uniform block allocated its generated points (5 GB) before any limit; nine GPU volume defects (below) | (1) to (7) fixed at the root with regression tests in tests/audit-132.test.js, each failing on the old code; the fallback test's scalar reference takes the same face rule and the head MRI vertex pin was re-pinned (positions and interior normals unchanged). A reported eighth item, under, over and NaN colours drawn opaque despite a volume opacity curve, is the tested contract (colors-browser.mjs: an all-negative MIP shows the under colour), not a defect; (8) ticks() looped forever when its step fell below the doubles' spacing (ticks(1e18, 1.0000000000000003e18)) or its span overflowed (ticks(-1e308, 1e308)), returned zeros below 1e-20 (toFixed(20)), nothing for subnormal or near-overflow spans, and labels that read alike past six digits; (9) dateTicks looped forever at the last Date, read years 0 to 99 as 1900 to 1999 and rolled negative years' months back a year; (10) histogram lost every value of a constant series at 1e16 and above, overflowed spans past the largest double and counted a value on an edge in the bin below; (11) Timeline.play threw from an already aborted signal instead of rejecting. (8) to (11) fixed at the root with tests in maths, scales, charts and updates.test.js that fail on the old code; (12) StatisticalChart.toSVG labelled its ticks with four significant digits, so ticks 1000.1 to 1000.5 all read 1000; (13) nearestPoint chained its half-pixel tie rule, so of five points 0.45 px apart it picked one 1.8 px away over an exact hit; (14) numberFormatter si clamped to yotta and then carried (1e30 read 1,000e27, 1e-30 read 0y), took its exponent from Math.log10 (inexact for subnormals) and its date mode threw on NaN, Infinity and values past a Date; (15) ChartPolish options such as `x: { domain: undefined }` replaced the default domain and threw a TypeError. (12) to (15) fixed at the root: labels come from ticks().labels; nearestPoint takes the closest distance first, then the point nearest the eye within half a pixel of it; SI exponents and digits are read from toExponential text, with q to Q prefixes and e notation beyond them, and the date mode prints a value no Date holds with String; defaults are spread before the options. Tests in stats.test.js, pick.test.js, chart-polish.test.js and chart-polish-browser.mjs (WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer) fail on the old code; (16) a StatisticalChart polygon split by NaN points closed only its last part; (17) ChartPolish drew no break marks for a secondaryY break, and drew a break's mask past the plot's edge when a zoom put the break outside it; (18) dateTicks gave no ticks for a range holding no whole second, min equal to max off a whole second included; (19) tilesIn read a box 360 degrees wide as one column, and tileLayer listed every tile before its maxTiles check, so a world area at zoom 24 (2^48 tiles) ran out of memory; (20) ForceLayout took a NaN or infinite temperature, an infinite length or gravity, non-finite start positions and NaN, infinite or negative weights, each giving NaN or infinite positions. (16) to (20) fixed at the root: each polygon part closes (SVG 2, 9.3.4); breaks are clipped to the visible bounds and a secondaryY break masks and marks the right axis only; ranges under a few seconds take 1, 2 or 5 times a power of ten milliseconds, labelled with the decimals the step needs and rounded with carry, and a zero-width range gets one tick, as ticks() does; tile ranges are counted before listing and a box 360 degrees or wider covers every column; the layout checks every option, position and weight. Tests in stats, scales, geo and graph.test.js and chart-polish-browser.mjs (WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer) fail on the old code; (21) a lone tick (ticks(5, 5), step 0) was labelled '5.000'; (22) tileAt and tileBounds took any zoom and tile index, returning half tiles for zoom 2.5 or edges past the map; (23) describeSeries and sonificationPlan overflowed on data or times spanning more than the largest double, giving a NaN mean and NaN pitches; (24) a TableDimension disposed after its CrossFilter threw, and one disposed before it sent two change events; (25) chart SVG, contour SVG and the shared XML writer copied C0 control characters, lone surrogates and U+FFFE/U+FFFF into documents XML 1.0 forbids them in, so a label read from data made the export unparsable; (26) GraphLayer took edges naming a node past the last, a negative or a fractional node, which the GPU then read outside the position buffer. (27) a 2D chart's repeated wheel zoom collapsed its window to zero width (1.3 to 1.3 after 400 steps; a pan then moved nothing) and a repeated zoom out overflowed to NaN; (28) mapColours gave every value the first colour (or NaN) for a colour range wider than the largest double; (29) extent took infinities, so one infinite colour value gave every finite value one end colour and one infinite position an infinite axis; (30) exported SVGs used fixed ids (sciviz-bar-0, sciviz-plot), so of two charts inlined in one page the second painted with the first's gradient and clip; (31) hex and the colour bar stops wrote a NaN channel as NaN (#NaNNaNNaN). (32) Figure.create left its new engine (configured canvas and GPU device) undisposed when the options were invalid (theme 'neon', a zero aspect, mode '4d', limits min above max); (33) Figure and setTheme took #rgb and #rrggbbaa chrome colours, which createTheme refuses, so the legend, tooltip and timeline panels (background plus alpha digits, '#fffd9') lost their background and an alpha background threw from the engine; (34) positions past the largest float32 (stored as infinity) stretched the fitted bounds to infinity, so nothing drew; (35) the legend and line swatch hex wrote NaN or out-of-range channels unclamped. (36) tileLayer added a layer to the renderer before the figure accepted it, so on a 2D chart (or a figure disposed during the load) it threw yet stayed drawn with its textures held. (37) attachAccessibility disposed the figure's working view before checking pageSize, so a refused call left the figure with none; (38) its focus marker showed for a 2D record outside the data window but inside the canvas, in the margin where the chart clips the mark away. (21) to (26) fixed at the root: a step of 0 labels the value's shortest exact text; tile helpers refuse a zoom outside whole 0 to 30 and an index outside the zoom's grid; the description scales the data by powers of two between a range pass and Welford's update (Welford 1962), exact for normal values, and the plan measures overflowing spans in halves; a dimension's dispose is idempotent, frees its rows with one event and only leaves a disposed filter; the shared escape replaces characters outside XML 1.0 section 2.2 Char with U+FFFD; GraphLayer checks edges with the layout's own check. (27) to (31) fixed at the root: zoom stops at 2^-17 of the window's largest coordinate (64 float32 steps, the GPU's plot precision) and zoom out and pan stop at the largest float32 (limits.md); colour fractions are taken from halved values, exact for normal doubles, so ordinary ranges keep their indices; extent skips non-finite values; SVG ids are a 32-bit FNV-1a hash of their definition, so equal ids mean equal definitions and the output stays deterministic; a NaN channel writes 00. Tests in figure-window.test.js and export-ids.test.js fail on the old code. (32) to (35): create disposes the engine when the constructor throws; the shared theme check requires #rrggbb for the four chrome colours, as createTheme did; positionBounds skips non-finite coordinates; every hex writer uses one clamped helper (packages/shared/srgb-hex.js). Tests in figure-create.test.js fail on the old code. (36): the figure takes the layer before the renderer does and a refused layer is disposed (geo.test.js, fails on the old code). Tiles above zoom 20 lost their shape in float32: each tile is now placed relative to its centre (Ohlarik 2008, "Precisions, Precisions", AGI Insight3D blog, read; Cozzi and Ring 2011, 3D Engine Design for Virtual Globes, ch. 5, not fetched): the CPU maps the centre and the grid offsets into the scene in double precision through the figure's double-precision transform, and the GPU adds the float32 offsets (geo-tiles-browser.mjs, WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer: zoom 22 and 24 draw the zoom 2 picture, 19,078 pixels differed on the old code). (37) and (38): options are checked before the old view goes, and the 2D marker hides outside the plot area (a11y.test.js, each part failing on the old code). (39) bar on a log axis took base 0 (or an infinite base on any axis), which maps to no plot position, so every bar was NaN and nothing drew without an error; (40) histogram and heatmap without values threw a bare TypeError from Float64Array.from or .length; (41) a disposed colour bar kept the 96 px right margin its 2D chart made for it and stayed in the export list. (39) to (41): the bar base must map to a finite plot position, missing values throw the coded TypeError, and the bar tells its figure when it is disposed, which drops it and gives back the default margin (figure-create.test.js, failing on the old code). (42) graphLayer added its layer to the renderer before the figure accepted it, so on a 2D chart it threw yet stayed drawn with its GPU buffers held; (43) one infinite node value made the graph's colour range infinite, so every other node took one end colour, and an options.range of NaN or min above max was taken; (44) boundsOf took infinite positions, so the framed graph was infinite and nothing drew; (45) nodeSize Infinity was taken. (42) to (45): the figure takes the layer first and a refused one is disposed, as tileLayer does; the range comes from extent (finite values) and rangeOption; boundsOf skips non-finite coordinates; nodeSize must be finite (graph.test.js, failing on the old code). (46) PNG and SVG export painted the legend swatches of a legend the page had hidden (visibility hidden, or a hidden ancestor) or detached, though its text was left out, and text in a visibility: collapse element was painted. (46): swatches follow the text runs' rules, and both treat any visibility but visible as hidden (color-scales.test.js, failing on the old code). (47) bindRasterAggregate's shade() kept options shadeAggregate refused, so after one bad call every pan or zoom refresh failed in shading; the binding now shades with the new options first and keeps them only on success (aggregate.test.js, failing on the old code). (48) shadeAggregate spread let the mark visited last in scan order colour pixels where two dilation discs overlapped, so a mirrored raster shaded differently; the dilation is now a grey dilation where the highest shaded level wins, independent of order, on the fixed radius and the density path alike (aggregate.test.js, failing on the old code). (49) figure.toSVG awaited only the axis updates while toPNG awaits ready(), so a raster SVG could be taken before the renderer's pipelines were ready and a renderer failure was not reported; toSVG now awaits ready() and rejects with that failure before drawing a frame (a11y.test.js, failing on the old code). (50) contourAMR took a dims of two or four sample counts and failed mid-sampling with a TypeError; it now refuses any dims that is not three counts with a coded RangeError (amr.test.js, failing on the old code). amr-data.ts, amr-readers.ts, amr.ts and amr-shader.ts read whole; no other defect found. (51) The axes awaited a second import of the lines package after their disposed check, so a figure disposed in that window still had its axis lines and tick labels drawn into the shared lines and labels plugins, where nothing removed them; the Line class now loads with the plugins and no await follows the check (axes-dispose.test.js disposes at every microtask of the update, failing on the old code). annotate.ts, annotate-math.ts, annotation-state.ts, appearance.ts, atmosphere.ts, axes.ts and axis-layout.ts read whole; no other defect found. (52) brickedVolume, gpuGraphLayer and globeAtmosphere added their layer to the renderer before the figure accepted it, so a refused layer (a 2D chart, a disposed figure) threw yet stayed drawn with its atlas, buffers or sky tables held; each figure now takes the layer first and a refused layer is disposed (bricks.test.js, graph.test.js and atmosphere.test.js, failing on the old code). bench.ts, bricks-plan.ts, bricks.ts and calc-expression.ts read whole; no other defect found. (53) traceAMR left a ray lying in the hierarchy's upper face (a zero direction component equal to the box's maximum) with no intervals, while sampleAMR returned the cells along it; both now follow the cell-centred convention of a clamped floor index, floor((x - lo) / dx) limited to n - 1: cells own [lo, hi), so every interior face has one owner, the upper one, and the hierarchy's upper faces are closed (amr.test.js checks rays in each upper face and an interior face against sampleAMR, failing on the old code). Turk et al. 2011 (yt, arXiv:1011.3514, read) states no face rule; Berger and Colella 1989 not fetched. (54) BrickedVolumeLayer.restore() and BrickedSliceLayer.restore() built new textures and buffers on the restored device without destroying the lost device's, so each restore left the old atlas, page table, transfer table and uniforms to the collector; restore now destroys them first (bricks.test.js counts live objects across restores, failing on the old code). (55) amrVolume never gave its layer to the figure: figure.layers did not list it and figure.dispose freed only its hidden source volume, so on a figure over a shared engine the AMR layer kept drawing with a disposed source; amrVolume now calls figure.addLayer before the renderer draws it, a refused layer frees its textures and source, its dispose leaves the figure, and figure.dispose empties its list before freeing so a layer that removes its own source frees it once (amr.test.js and amr-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code). (56) An expression's named array called value, coords, x, y or z was silently replaced by the built-in variable of that name, so 'x' over an array named x returned the coordinate (and backticks could not reach the array); calcGrid now refuses such an array with a coded error naming it, and elevation, which reads only coordinates, ignores named arrays (calc.test.js, failing on the old code). calc.ts read whole; VTK wedge, pyramid and tetrahedron corner orders checked against the VTK class descriptions (read); no other defect found. (57) convertCFUnits took a plural on any unit, so the UDUNITS symbols ms (millisecond), Pas (pascal second) and Ns (newton second) read as metre, pascal and newton, and an Object prototype key such as constructor or toString read as a unit and gave NaN; a plural is now taken only on a unit name (meter, second, hour, day, kelvin, pascal, bar, millibar, radian, degree and the like) and only the vocabulary's own keys are units. (58) readCFField read a unitless longitude or latitude in degrees but its cell bounds with no units, so a field with bounds threw where the same field without bounds read. (59) conservativeCF took the shorter arc between a longitude cell's two bounds, so a cell wider than 180 degrees ([0, 200] centred at 100) became its complement and a remap of two such cells gave 1.5 for 1.444; the arc holding the cell's own centre is now taken (CF 1.12 section 7.1). (57) to (59) fixed at the root with tests in cf.test.js, each failing on the old code. cf-units.ts, cf.ts, cf-grid.ts and cf-vertical.ts read whole; the Appendix D formulas checked against CF 1.12 (read; ocean_sigma_z takes sigma where it is defined and zlev elsewhere, nsigma deprecated, as implemented); no other defect found. (60) calcGrid quality took a quadrilateral's corner Jacobian as an unsigned area (its corner normal was the corner's own cross product), so a concave corner read positive and a dart [0,0], [2,0], [2,2], [1.5,0.5] scored 1; the Verdict library (Stimpson et al. 2007, SAND2007-1751, section 5.6 and eqs. 7 to 13, read) signs each corner area by the centre normal X1 x X2, and calcGrid now does, so the dart scores -2 and a symmetric bow tie 0 (calc.test.js, failing on the old code; docs/sciviz-analysis.md). Triangles stay unsigned, as in Verdict without a surface normal. (61) charts-extra spread whole data arrays into Math.max and a rest parameter, so polarChart over a million points and candlestickChart over 300,000 rows threw the call stack's uncoded RangeError; a date axis past the last Date threw toISOString's uncoded RangeError; radar, polar and calendar scale labels used toPrecision, so a radar domain [1000, 1001] labelled five steps 1.0e+3 and a polar radius 1234 read 1.23e+3; and its own escape copied C0 controls into the SVG, the class (25) fixed elsewhere. Inputs are now scanned by loops, a date x is checked against +/-8.64e15, labels take the significant digits their step needs in plain decimals, and text goes through the shared XML 1.0 escape (charts-extra.test.js, failing on the old code). chart-data.ts, cf-vertical.ts and charts-extra.ts read whole. (62) cinematicVolume took any volume layer: one removed from the figure was hidden and drew nothing without an error, and one held by another figure bound that figure's device textures, a GPU validation error; it now refuses a volume its figure does not hold before hiding it or allocating (cinematic.test.js, failing on the old code). cinematic.ts and cinematic-shader.ts read whole (Henyey-Greenstein sampling and the scattering angle sign checked; the unnormalised phase matches the single-scatter march's light scale, a convention, not changed). (63) readCGNS took any coordinate arrays: CoordinateR without CoordinateTheta threw a bare TypeError, CoordinateX beside CoordinateTheta read the angle as y, a spherical set (R, Theta, Phi) read as cylindrical with z = 0, and a short CoordinateY or CoordinateZ read 0 past its end; it now takes one complete Cartesian (X, Y, Z) or cylindrical (R, Theta, Z) set of equal lengths (CGNS SIDS section 7.1, from the record) and refuses any other with a coded error (cgns-coords.test.js over an in-memory SIDS tree, failing on the old code). cgns.ts read whole; element type numbers checked against the SIDS ElementType_t list. (64) readCFVertical compared the ocean double sigma level index from 0 with k_c, so k_c = 2 put three levels in the upper sigma set. CF Appendix D (1.12) does not state the base of k. The formula came into CF from the 2001 proposal of the Harvard Ocean Prediction System (HOPS) double sigma (R. Signell to the CF list, 23 August 2001, https://web.mit.edu/phaley/www/Work/HOPS/netCDFconventions_email.html, read), written z(i,j,k) in Fortran order; its sibling NCOM sigma over z formula in the same mail reads 'for k <= nsigma' with nsigma the count of sigma layers, which holds only for k = 1 at the first level, and the CF discussion that replaced that test in CF 1.9 (cf-conventions issue 314, read) took k = 1 as the surface level. The CDL zero base in the CF terminology describes ncdump index notation, not an integer term's meaning. So k counts from 1 and k_c is the number of upper levels (cf.test.js, failing on the old code); the two-fold sigma system behind HOPS (Deleersnijder and Beckers, On the use of the sigma-coordinate system in regions of large bathymetric variations, Journal of Marine Systems 3(4-5), 381 to 390, 1992, author version https://orbi.uliege.be/handle/2268/14534, read) is continuous and names no level index. Unchanged and recorded in docs/limits.md: read with heights (z1, z2 and f negative), the CF lower-set formula f + (sigma - 1)(depth - f) does not reach -depth at sigma = 0; it is consistent only in HOPS depths (positive down, sigma 0 to 1 above the interface and 1 to 2 below). Margo evaluates the CF text as written. (65) The cinematic path tracer's Henyey-Greenstein phase lacked its 1/(4 pi), so it integrated to 4 pi over the sphere, and the single-scatter march used an implicit phase of 1; the two agreed only with `light` read as irradiance divided by 4 pi, a unit no other Margo volume path uses. Both now use the phase normalised over the sphere (Pharr, Jakob and Humphreys, Physically Based Rendering, 4th ed., 2023, section 11.3, https://pbr-book.org/4ed/Volume_Scattering/Phase_Functions, read), `light` is the distant light's irradiance and its default moves from 0.7 to 8.8 (0.7 times 4 pi) so the default image is unchanged. HG sampling checked against the inverse CDF: z = (1 + g^2 - s^2) / (2g), s = (1 - g^2) / (1 - g + 2g x), forward for positive g, so the sampled-direction weight is exactly 1. cinematic-browser.mjs pins the march and the path tracer to the same thin-slab single-scatter integral and the g = 0.6 back-scatter to (1 - g^2) / (1 + g)^3 of it, on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer (fails on the old code: 111 against 73). (66) clip.ts read whole: clipGrid with a box shape and no bounds, and thresholdGrid or isoVolume (also through clipOperator) with no range, threw a bare TypeError; each is now the coded error the other malformed inputs get (clip.test.js, failing on the old code). Checked: the cut keeps VTK tetra orientation (base normal toward the fourth point), the six box halfspaces give disjoint complement pieces, isoVolume's inverted upper cut keeps the inclusive interval, and connectivity's union-find roots are each region's lowest cell, so 'first' order and size ties agree with the docs. (67) Box crinkle kept only cells with positive volume in the exact six-halfspace cut, so a cell that only touched the box (a shared face, edge or corner) was dropped while plane, sphere and scalar crinkle keep any cell with a retained point. It now keeps a cell with a retained point, the rule of the other shapes and of VTK's extract-geometry with boundary cells (Kitware, vtkExtractGeometry class reference, behaviour only, from the record, not fetched this pass), and still keeps a cell holding a box no vertex sees (clip.test.js, failing on the old code: a unit cube with bounds [1,2,0,1,0,1] gave no cell). (68) cloud-codecs.ts, cloud-lz4-java.ts and cloud-precomputed.ts read whole: LZ4 block bounds, BloscLZ far distances, Blosc1 split and byte/bit unshuffle (pinned by the numcodecs fixtures), the 28-bit seeded xxHash32 of the N5 Java block stream, the compressed-segmentation block and table offsets and the sharded index (compressed Morton key, minishard offset deltas from the end of the previous chunk) agree with their specifications. cloud-store.ts: an HTTP store built from a URL with a query (a signed URL or an Azure SAS token on the container) dropped the query from every object request, because relative URL resolution discards the base query, so each read failed with 403; the query is now kept (omezarr.test.js, failing on the old code: the server saw /root/c/0 without ?sig=a%2Fb&sv=1). (69) cloud-zarr.ts, cloud-volumes.ts, contour.ts, contour2d.ts, contour2d-triangulate.ts, cubes.ts, curvilinear-grid.ts and curvilinear-shader.ts read whole, no defect found. Checked: sharded Zarr v3 slots are C order over the chunks per shard with the all-ones pair as a missing chunk, OME-NGFF scale and translation compose dataset before group transforms, N5 blocks are big-endian with the x axis fastest, precomputed chunk names add voxel_offset and JPEG chunks are x by y times z planes, the tetrahedron and triangle gradients use the dual edge vectors, the square asymptotic decider pairs crossings by the sign of v0 v2 - v1 v3, the Lawson flip and Bowyer-Watson cavity keep counter-clockwise triangles with an exact incircle sign, the marching cubes table closes every loop because each crossed edge is walked once each way, and the trilinear Newton step solves J delta = r by Cramer's rule on CPU and GPU. The N5 specification lists gzip with level only; the useZlib flag is covered in (71). (70) curvilinear.ts: a grid whose cell tree overran the 2D texture limit (about four million cells on a 4096 compatibility device) threw only after the hidden source volume had been added to the figure and the uniform buffer and earlier textures created, leaving all of them behind; the limit is now checked for every topology texture before anything is created (curvilinear.test.js, failing on the old code with a 4-texel device: the source layer and GPU calls were made before the throw). (71) cloud-volumes.ts: an N5 dataset whose gzip compression attributes carry useZlib: true (the N5 Java writer's gzip option, which emits an RFC 1950 zlib stream instead of an RFC 1952 gzip member) failed every block with an incorrect-header inflate error; such blocks now inflate as zlib, false or absent keeps gzip, and a non-boolean useZlib is refused at open with a coded error (omezarr.test.js, hand-built zlib and gzip blocks, failing on the old code). Sources: N5 specification README (saalfeldlab/n5, the format text only), RFC 1950 and RFC 1952 (Deutsch and Gailly, 1996). (72) curvilinear.ts from its prepare step on (uniform packing, section and outline meshes, render pipelines, restore and dispose) read whole, no defect found: the four clip planes end before the section colour slot, a fractional slice interpolates neighbouring coordinate surfaces, and a restore rebinds every section. cvd-data.ts and cvd.ts: the three full-severity matrices match Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes (2009, IEEE TVCG 15(6)) Table 1, applied to linear light; cvdMatrix read its table with a plain property lookup, so an inherited name such as 'constructor' or '__proto__' crashed with an uncoded TypeError inside the interpolation instead of the coded unknown-type error; the lookup now takes own keys only (color-scales.test.js, failing on the old code). (73) datasets.ts: a polyline, polyvertex or triangle strip that repeated a point was refused as a repeated cell index, so a closed polyline (first point repeated at the end, as contour and stripper outputs write loops) or a strip with a turn-around could not be read; these three chain cells now check only their point range, a closed loop draws its closing segment, a zero-length polyline step and a strip triangle with a repeated point are skipped, and a cell with nothing left to draw is still refused (datasets.test.js, failing on the old code). The rest of datasets.ts (structured, rectilinear and polydata linearisation, the polygon ear clipping and polyhedron fan or decomposition, composites) read whole, no other defect. (74) datasets-readers.ts, datasets-polyhedron.ts and datasets-elements.ts read whole, no defect found. Checked: PolyData cells are numbered verts, lines, polys then strips as their cell data is; VTK pixel and voxel corners reorder to quad and hexahedron (0,1,3,2 and 0,1,3,2,4,5,7,6); polyhedron faces end at their faceoffsets entry, with -1 for other cells; the mean-value weights follow Ju, Schaefer and Warren (2005, ACM TOG 24(3)) equations 10 and 11, including the on-triangle barycentric branch and the coplanar-outside skip; the inside test sums oriented solid angles (Van Oosterom and Strackee, 1983, IEEE TBME 30(2)); the 15-node wedge corner function 0.5 L (1 + s t)(2L - 2 + s t) equals the textbook L(2L - 1)(1 + s t)/2 - L(1 - t^2)/2; the tetrahedron midpoint split cuts its octahedron on one diagonal into four tetrahedra; integration takes the absolute Jacobian, so tetrahedron winding is free. The Lagrange hexahedron edge question left open here is settled in (75). (75) datasets-readers.ts: a Lagrange hexahedron (VTK type 72) from a VTK XML file older than version 2.2 read edges 10 and 11 in the new order, so a 27-node cell from such a file took the node on edge 3-7 for edge 2-6 and the reverse and drew a twisted element whose field missed the source polynomial (x y + z^2 - 2 x z read 0 where the point gave 0.0117). VTK 9.1 release notes, Data section (raw.githubusercontent.com/Kitware/VTK/master/Documentation/release/9.1.md, fetched): the VTK_LAGRANGE_HEXAHEDRON numbering was corrected to match VTK_QUADRATIC_HEXAHEDRON for the quadratic cell, the (0, 1) and (1, 1) edges swap (nodes 18 and 19 trade places in its before and after drawing), and readers convert data to the new numbering by XML file version 2.2 and legacy file version 5.1; the WriteVTK discussion of the 27-node hexahedron (discourse.julialang.org/t/93698, fetched) shows the same two mid-edge nodes trading places between version 1.0 and 2.2 files. The vtkHigherOrderHexahedron class reference (vtk.org/doc/nightly/html/classvtkHigherOrderHexahedron.html, fetched) names a VTK 8 to 9 numbering map without describing it; no VTK source was read. The reader now takes the VTKFile version attribute: below 2.2, or absent, the node blocks of edges 10 and 11 (order - 1 nodes each, after the 8 corners) are swapped back; 2.2 and later read as written; a version that is not major.minor is refused. Types 25 and 29 (quadratic and triquadratic hexahedra) were never renumbered and are untouched. The legacy .vtk reader (grids.ts) reads no Lagrange cells, so its 5.1 rule has nothing to convert. datasets.test.js checks versions 0.1, 1.0, 2.1 and absent against the old order, 2.2, 2.10 and 3.0 against the new, and an order 3 cell (two nodes per edge) read from a 2.1 and a 2.2 file; it fails on the old code. (76) density-shaders.ts, density.ts, derive-shader.ts and derive.ts read whole, no defect found. Checked: the count shader drops non-finite points, points behind the eye and outside the depth range, and maps NDC to the pixel row from the top; the draw opacity 1 - exp(-k c) with k = -log1p(-a) equals 1 - (1 - a)^c, opacity 1 draws any count opaque; the Gaussian is normalised over all taps, so the plot edge is zero-extended as documented; an aborted readCounts leaves the map promise handled by Promise.race. derive: second-order centred and one-sided differences (-3f0 + 4f1 - f2)/2h, the two-point secant, row-major J_ij = du_i/dx_j, curl (J21 - J12, J02 - J20, J10 - J01), Q = -tr(J^2)/2 = (|Omega|^2 - |S|^2)/2 and lambda2 the middle eigenvalue of (J^2 + (J^2)^T)/2 = S^2 + Omega^2 (Jeong and Hussain 1995, J. Fluid Mech. 285, from the record); the Jacobi rotation t = sgn(theta)/(|theta| + sqrt(theta^2 + 1)) with theta = (d - a)/2b and its updates follow the classical cyclic Jacobi method (Golub and Van Loan, Matrix Computations, section 8.5, from the record), CPU and WGSL alike. (77) engineering-common.ts and exodus.ts: the Exodus reader passed each element's connectivity straight to the shared VTK-order element builder, so HEX20 and HEX27 took the vertical mid-edge nodes for the top ones and the reverse, HEX27 also took its centroid and face centres in the wrong places, and WEDGE15 swapped its vertical and top mid-edge nodes; a 20-node unit cube with nodal f = x y + z^2 - 2 x z read -0.0469 where the point gave -0.0344. Source: Schoof and Yarberry, EXODUS II: A Finite Element Data Model, SAND92-2137 (1992), Figure 4 node ordering and the side set table (fetched, nschloe.github.io/meshio/exodus.pdf, page 10 read as an image): HEX nodes 9-12 bottom, 13-16 vertical (1-5, 2-6, 3-7, 4-8), 17-20 top; WEDGE 7-9 bottom, 10-12 vertical, 13-15 top; TETRA 5-10 and the TRUSS mid node as VTK. SEACAS element types page and its hex27 and pyramid figures (sandialabs.github.io/seacas-docs, fetched): HEX27 node 21 the centroid, 22 bottom, 23 top, 24 x-min, 25 x-max, 26 y-min, 27 y-max face centres; PYRAMID13 as VTK. No WEDGE18 figure or text is published there, so WEDGE18 is now refused with a coded error rather than read in a guessed order. exodus.ts renumbers HEX20, HEX27 and WEDGE15 before the element builder. engineering.test.js writes one-element classic NetCDF Exodus files and checks every tessellated point's field against f and the volume (1 for the hexahedra, 0.5 for the wedge); it fails on the old code. (78) ensight.ts element numbering: tria6, quad8, tetra10, pyramid13, penta15 and hexa20 connectivity goes to the shared VTK-order element builder unchanged, and that is correct. Source: EnSight 7 User Manual, chapter 11, Figure 11-1 (vis.lbl.gov UM-C11.pdf, page 11-4, read as an image; the link no longer resolves): corners first, then the mid-edge nodes in VTK's order (hexa20 nodes 9-12 bottom, 13-16 top, 17-20 vertical; penta15 7-9 bottom, 10-12 top, 13-15 vertical; tria6, quad8, tetra10 and pyramid13 as VTK), so EnSight needs no renumbering, unlike Exodus. The bar3 was wrong: that figure draws the three node bar with node 2 in the middle, and the Ansys EnSight User Manual 2025 R2, section 10.2.4, Figure 10.1 (ansyshelp.ansys.com, v252 ensight_um, fetched, read as an image) draws it the same way, but the reader passed nodes 1 2 3 to the VTK quadratic edge, whose mid node is last, so a bar3 through (0, 0), (1, 1), (2, 0) drew from (0, 0) to (1, 1) bending out through (2, 0) (a tessellated point at x = 1.375 sat at y = -0.125 instead of 0.859). ensight.ts now passes nodes 1 3 2; engineering.test.js checks every tessellated point on the parabola and its nodal field, failing on the old code. No VTK or ParaView source was read. (79) exodus.ts rest, export-load.ts, export-media.ts, export-vector.ts, extent.ts, geo-elevation.ts and geo-lod-plan.ts read whole. One defect: the Exodus reader took a block's ID from eb_prop1 at its position among the blocks that have a connectN variable, but eb_prop1, connectN and vals_elem_varNebM all count every block, a null block (eb_status 0, no connectN) included, so a file whose first block is null gave the second block the first block's ID (eb_prop1 10, 20 read 10 for the block that is 20). exodus.ts now indexes eb_prop1 by the connectN number (engineering.test.js, failing on the old code). Checked without defect: the combined vals_nod_var layout (time, variable, node), the per-block element result names, global variables and set names; the vector export's clip-space clipping against reversed-Z planes 0 <= z <= w, perspective-correct colour averaging (RGBA/w over 1/w), back-to-front BSP order for reversed-Z, stroke and point widths in CSS pixels; extent and positionBounds skip non-finite values; Terrarium (R 256 + G + B / 256 - 32768, Mapzen 2016), quantized-mesh rasterised at pixel centres, and the tile selection's frustum test and skirt winding. (80) geo-lod-cache.ts, geo-lod.ts, geo-time-data.ts, geo-time.ts, geo-time-raster.ts, geo-topology.ts, geo-trips.ts, geo-vector-math.ts, geo-vector-data.ts and geo-vector.ts lines 1 to 216 read whole, no defect. Checked: the TopoJSON specification quantized points are absolute while arcs are delta coded and an arc index ~i walks arc i reversed; pointy-top hex axial rounding (cube rounding) and the great-circle path's axis for antipodal ends; antimeridian unwrapping and strip clipping of vector features, with project() wrapping longitude so labels across the dateline land in place. (81) geo-vector.ts lines 216 to 403, geo-sgp4.ts, geo-sgp4-deep.ts, glyph.ts, graph-gpu-types.ts, graph-gpu-data.ts, graph-gpu-reference.ts and graph-gpu-shaders.ts read whole. SGP4 and SDP4 checked term by term against Vallado, Crawford, Hujsak and Kelso, Revisiting Spacetrack Report #3 (AIAA 2006-6753, fetched from CelesTrak, Appendix D and E only; Appendix F code not read): all 29 Appendix D element sets match every Appendix E state within 0.1 mm and 0.1 mm/s, the decay sets stop where the listing ends; Hoots and Roehrich, Spacetrack Report #3 (1980) itself was not reread. Two ForceAtlas2 defects against Jacomy, Venturini, Heymann and Bastian, PLoS ONE 9(6) e98679, 2014 (fetched, read): with overlap prevention the repulsion was delta scaling m1 m2 / d'^2 (magnitude d / d' times the paper's scaling m1 m2 / d') and an overlapping pair repelled with 100 scaling m1 m2 d instead of the constant 100 scaling m1 m2, so nearly coincident overlapping nodes barely separated (two nodes 0.001 apart with size 1 moved 0.0008 instead of 0.124 in the first step), and attraction used d instead of d'; and the global speed was tolerance^2 traction / swinging where the paper's equation 14 is tolerance traction / swinging (tolerance 0.5 gave 0.125 instead of 0.25). The CPU reference and the GPU force, attraction and speed shaders now follow the paper, touching borders (d' = 0) exert no force (graph-gpu.test.js, failing on the old code; graph-gpu-browser.mjs matches GPU to reference on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer). Checked without defect: SGP4 TLE columns, checksum and two-digit years, OMM frame checks, IAU 1982 sidereal time and TEME to geodetic; glyph orientation quaternions (+X onto the vector) and the built-in shapes' rotation into +X; warp by vector and scalar; FA2 repulsion, linear, LinLog and dissuade-hubs attraction, gravity, strong gravity, local speed and the overlap tenth. (82) graph-gpu-bundle.ts read whole, no defect: edge compatibility (angle, scale, position, visibility), the per-segment spring constant K / (|P| x segments), the 1/d electrostatic pull weighted by compatibility, P0 = 1 subdivision point doubled per cycle with the step halved and iterations times 2/3, and arc-length resampling between cycles match Holten and van Wijk, Force-Directed Edge Bundling for Graph Visualization, EuroVis 2009 (fetched, read); the default step 0.1 differs from the paper's S0 = 0.04 and is documented as Margo's default. (83) glyph.ts: scaleMode 'components' on a planar field (vector z = 0) dropped every glyph because any zero scale component skipped the sample; a zero component now flattens the glyph along that local axis (VTK and ParaView glyph filters keep such degenerate glyphs; no VTK or ParaView source read), each projected triangle taking the axis normal signed by its projected winding, the limit of the inverse-transpose rule, so the flat glyph is lit from both sides without a NaN normal; only an all-zero scale omits the sample (glyph.test.js and glyph-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code). (84) graph-gpu.ts read whole, no defect: the storage-limit check covers the largest buffer of each kind (positions, keys, tree, adjacency, which outsizes edge data), the tree levels run leaves to root with one copy per level, the dispatch grid covers every invocation in 65536-wide rows, and the cooling floor, run() cancellation and disposal hold. graph-gpu-draw.ts read whole, three defects fixed (graph-gpu-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, each failing on the old code): arrowheads ended at the target node's centre, so a node hid most of the arrow (4 visible pixels for a 12 px arrow on a 24 px node), and now end at its border; in 3D an edge with one end behind the camera was dropped whole, and is now trimmed to the near plane (reversed depth, z = w) so its visible part draws; node labels painted on a CSS-pixel canvas, blurred on high-density displays, and now paint at the device pixel ratio. (85) graph.ts read whole against Fruchterman and Reingold 1991 and the Barnes and Hut opening test with Barnes's 1994 offset (as cited in the file; papers not refetched): one defect, the GPU storage check covered only the edge buffer, so a graph with many nodes and few edges passed it and failed later in buffer creation without a coded error; it now checks positions and values too (graph.test.js, failing on the old code). grids.ts read whole (cell faces and edges, surface extraction, plane slicing, the legacy VTK reader to version 5.1, the mesh layer): one defect, the mesh layer's automatic colour range took an infinite value as its end, pushing every finite value to one end colour, and a range option accepted an infinite end; both now use finite values only (mesh-ownership.test.js, failing on the old code). The README said parseVtk refuses version 5.1 files, though it reads them; corrected. (86) graph-gpu-draw.ts labels: placeGraphLabels put every label 6 CSS px from its node's centre whatever the node size, so a label sat on any node wider than 12 px; a label box now takes a radius (the node's projected radius, default 2, so the old 6 px offset holds) and starts 4 px beyond it, and the GPU graph layer passes half each labelled node's nodeSize, the radius its node and arrowhead shaders draw (graph-gpu.test.js, failing on the old code; graph-gpu-browser.mjs checks a 40 px node's label clears its border on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer). (87) index.ts, layer.ts, markup.ts and motion-state.ts read whole, no defect. interact.ts read whole, two defects: a box or lasso drag followed every pointer, so a second finger or pen moved its corner and its release ended the selection, and a figure without camera controls left the canvas's touch-action alone, so a touch drag in box or lasso mode scrolled the page and the browser cancelled the selection; the drag now follows only the pointer that started it, and a selection mode sets touch-action none, restored on returning to pan or orbit and on dispose (pick-gpu-hover.test.js, failing on the old code). (88) labels-shader.ts read whole, no defect (label lookup by nearest voxel on the same vertex-centred grid the linear sampler uses, outline test per neighbour, front-to-back over and optical-depth mixing). link-layer.ts read whole, one defect: a groups binding (histogram or heatmap bins) selected the bin under any click, so the click that ends a pan or a box drag released over a bin replaced the selection with that bin; a press that travels more than 6 CSS px, the orbit controls' click radius, is now a drag and selects nothing (link-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code). (89) math-parser.ts read whole, one defect: an escaped delimiter after \left or \right (\{, \} or \|, as in a cases brace) kept its backslash, so the MathML showed a backslash and a brace and the outline label looked up a glyph for it; they now map to the brace or double bar TeX draws (math.test.js, failing on the old code). (90) media-tiles.ts at devicePixelRatio 2: GPU graph nodes and edges scaled their CSS pixel sizes by window.devicePixelRatio, so a 20 px node drew 40 px on a canvas the engine backs at 1 pixel per CSS pixel and in a scale 1 export, and half size in a scale 2 export at ratio 1; the frame uniform now carries the target's pixels per CSS pixel (SciRenderer.cssScale, the export's scale while tiles draw) and the graph reads it (pixel-density-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code). Scatter points draw in target pixels on screen and CSS pixels in export, which differ only on a 2x backing store (light tier): docs/known-bugs.md. (91) labels-shader.ts mixing, analysed against WGSL 15.7.5: one defect, reachable for two alphas up to 2^-25 even with an exact log (the union alpha 1 - (1 - a)(1 - b) rounded to 0, so over divided by 0 and the optical weights were 0 / 0), and below about 5e-7 under log's 2^-21 absolute error (weights of either sign); the opaque clamp let 4% of a half-transparent sample through an opaque one. The union alpha is now a + b (1 - a), optical depths below 1/16 a Taylor series, a subnormal alpha zero, opaque samples their exact limit (labels.test.js float32 edge sweep, failing on the old code). (92) media-tiles.ts read whole, no further defect (a size whose aspect differs from the canvas stretches the layout, as docs/sciviz-media.md states). media-containers.ts read whole, no reachable defect (webpMetadata keeps a source VP8X's XMP and EXIF flag bits when it drops those chunks, but its only input is the browser encoder, which writes neither). molecule.ts, molecule-data.ts, molecule-render.ts, monitor-sweep.ts (a late rejection after a deadline is handled by the race) and mrc.ts read whole, no defect.  Tests in maths, geo, a11y, table, graph and contour2d.test.js and tests/shared-xml-document.test.js fail on the old code Pass from the end of the alphabet: transferEditor left its section in the parent and a change listener on the function when its volume binding could not apply (a disposed layer) or its first draw failed (no 2D context); creation now undoes itself and checks the histogram before building anything (transfer.test.js, failing on the old code). The tetrahedral volume clamped each node value to the classifier range before interpolating, so a cell whose values crossed the range drew the interpolation of clamped values (a ramp -1 to 1 over range 0 to 1 read 0.5, grey 103, at its centre where the field is 0), and it bound a colorScale map's float table as a filterable texture, a GPU validation error that drew nothing (under colour lost). Values are now normalised unclamped, as the regular volume stores them, and a colour-scale volume reads its table through the shared lookup (tetvolume-browser.mjs, WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code; entry 6403 to 7030 B, ceiling raised to 7286 B in sizes.json). The curvilinear volume bound its transfer texture the same way (curvilinear.ts binding 2), so a colorScale map drew nothing behind a GPU validation error in the ray caster and in its sections; it now takes the same specialised lookup (curvilinear-browser.mjs, WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code). groupFileSeries took the last digit run anywhere in a basename, so a digit in the extension split HDF5, NetCDF-4 and similar sequences into one-file groups (data_010.h5 and data_011.h5 read as two files numbered 5); it now takes the last number before a letter-led extension (or .nii.gz-style extensions) and falls back to the last digit run for names such as foo.vtk.2 (temporal.test.js, failing on the old code; docs/sciviz-temporal.md). VolumeQuad.create disposed only the layout when its first crosshair failed, leaving the quad's resize observer, canvas click listeners and signal listener live (a click restarted slice work on the dead figures); it now disposes the quad (views-quad-create.test.js, failing on the old code). transferEditor picked a 2D widget by its lower-left corner, so a click inside a large rectangle selected (and a drag moved) a nearby small widget; it now picks by distance to the drawn shape (0 inside a rectangle, ellipse or triangle), then by distance to the centre among shapes holding the click (transfer.test.js, failing on the old code). scalarMesh (the surface and mesh vector export) took (v - r0) / (r1 - r0), which overflows for a range wider than the largest double; it now takes halved values as mapColours does (export-ids.test.js, failing on the old code). The GPU draw of such a range coloured every value NaN: the surface, mesh and graph shaders took the range as float32, so [-1e308, 1e308] became [-Infinity, Infinity]; each now takes rangeUniform's words (the range start held in float32, the reciprocal half span and the fraction at the start, computed in doubles) through the WGSL colourFraction. The same draw found two more: the surface's normal matrix was the inverse scale, 0 for an axis whose scale rounds to 0 in float32 (a z range past the double limit), so every normal was NaN; it is now the cofactor matrix, which points along that axis. dataToScene took (lo + hi) / 2 and hi - lo, Infinity for bounds such as [1e308, 1.7e308], and now takes halved bounds (range-overflow-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, and range-overflow.test.js, each failing on the old code). readInWorker's signal walk, the reader wire encoder and readerBuffers recursed into a cyclic argument or result until the stack overflowed (an uncoded RangeError); each now keeps its ancestor path and refuses a cycle with a coded error, while a shared acyclic object is still copied (workers.test.js, failing on the old code). tensor-track.ts read line by line: hyperstreamlines flipped the second and third eigenvectors together to keep the second's sign, but the decomposition is right-handed, so a sign change of the principal axis flipped the third and mirrored the ring, twisting the tube (29 of 8,192 segments on a circular fiber); each transverse axis now keeps its own sign (tensor.test.js, failing on the old code). Four more hyperstreamline defects, each with a tensor.test.js case failing on the old code: the last ring of each segment took the new ring's FA, so the colour stepped at every ring instead of running along the tube (each ring now keeps its own FA); the tube sampled with sampleTensor, which gives nothing when any interpolation neighbour is masked, so it stopped beside a masked cell while its fiber went on (the tube now reweights the valid neighbours; sampleTensor keeps its documented null); a valid tensor interpolated with one of the other sign can sum to 0, and the ellipse radii divided by that largest eigenvalue gave NaN vertices (such a ring is now a point); a field whose bounds span more than the largest double gave an infinite voxel spacing and is now refused with the field's coded error. tensor-glyph.ts read line by line: the superquadric exponents, Westin cl/cp, the planar axis swap, the normal transform and the bounds agree with Kindlmann 2004; no defect. temporal.ts read line by line: no defect; a field whose values differ by more than the largest double is refused with a coded error (docs/limits.md). table-read.ts read line by line: CSV inference took an integer column with a value past the safe range as int64 even when a value did not fit in 64 bits, then refused the file; such a column is now numbers (table.test.js, failing on the old code). Judged against pandas read_csv: a fully blank line was refused as a row with the wrong field count, and nan/inf cells (numpy.savetxt) made a numeric column text; blank and whitespace-only lines are now skipped and those cells read as NaN/+-Infinity (table.test.js, failing on the old code). tensor-glyph.ts: stride stepped the linear sample index, so a regular field kept whole rows unevenly; it now steps every axis like ParaView Extract Subset (tensor-browser.mjs on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer, failing on the old code). table.ts, table-batches.ts read line by line: no defect. table-aggregate.ts: bins came from floor((v - lo) / (hi - lo) * bins), so a value on a drawn edge lo + i w landed one bin low about one time in seven (0.175 in [0.1, 0.7] over 8 bins), and a finite range wider than the largest double put every value in bin 0; the edges now decide the bin and wide ranges use halved bounds. table-arrow.ts: Time and Duration columns whose writer omitted the unit (the Schema.fbs default, MILLISECOND) were read as seconds. table-parquet.ts: an all-null page that carries its encoding header (dictionary bit width, delta block header, as writers emit) was refused as trailing values, and an RLE run longer than the page's values added rows instead of being refused (table.test.js with hand-built files, each failing on the old code). Parquet FLOAT16, JSON and ENUM columns read as hex text (now read per the format's LogicalTypes.md, UUID as 8-4-4-4-12 text). table-aggregate.ts: the GPU kernel kept 80 KB of function arrays at 4,096 bins (WGSL 2.4 caps them at 8,192 bytes) and ran on the CPU on the WebGL2 layer above 204 bins; now one invocation per (tile, bin) run (table-browser.mjs at 4,096 bins on both backends). surface.ts: infinite heights drew as spikes rather than holes, and setHeights after dispose wrote a destroyed buffer (surface-repeat-browser.mjs, updates.test.js). stream.ts: a 3D live trace appended without z put the new samples at z = 0, a second streamLayer on a layer still binding was accepted, and an abort while binding left the layer claimed for good; stream-sources.ts: eventSource refused text/event-stream with whitespace before its parameters or as the last of repeated values (stream.test.js, each failing on the old code). spec.ts: validateFigureSpec threw an uncoded TypeError for a timeline without frames or a null mutation, and checked timeline frames only after fromJSON had allocated the first figure; spec-schema.ts allowed 5 limits, which the loader refuses (spec.test.js, failing on the old code). spec-values.ts: a $transfer without a range crashed with an uncoded TypeError and one with an infinite range restored a NaN transfer, a numeric $date restored as a 1970 instant, and a $colormap without a kind or name was accepted (spec.test.js, failing on the old code). spec-values.ts: a scalar -0 saved as 0 because JSON has no -0 (it is now tagged $number "-0", like NaN and the infinities; typed arrays already kept it in their bytes), and an $array with an unknown dtype and a wrong hash or bad base64 reported the hash or base64 instead of the dtype (the dtype is now checked first; spec.test.js, failing on the old code). share-embed.ts and share.ts: an embed origin that is not a URL at all ("*", "null", a number) threw an uncoded "Invalid URL", and a client request timeout above 2,147,483,647 ms fired after 1 ms because timers hold a signed 32-bit delay (now a coded range error, documented as 1 to 2,147,483,647); readShareURL gave an uncoded, empty TypeError for data that is not deflate (cancelling the errored stream replaced the error), a SyntaxError for deflated text that is not JSON, a property-read TypeError for JSON null and an uncoded "Invalid URL", and read the shared array table before checking the version (all now coded; applyShareView with no view object is coded too; share.test.js, failing on the old code). series-spectral.ts: addSpectrogramView passed half-cell edge bounds as the heatmap's x and y, which name the first and last cell centres, so every cell was drawn wider and shifted half a hop and half a bin (series.test.js, failing on the old code). series-source.ts: readSeries inferred each CSV chunk's column kinds on their own, so a column could be text in one chunk and numbers in the next; kinds are now kept from the first chunk where a column has a value, as Apache Arrow's streaming CSV reader does (series.test.js, failing on the old code). series-figure.ts read, no defect found. scientific-bytes.ts: readFITS and readMRC cancelled the gzip stream even after it had failed, so a truncated or damaged gzip file (or the 4 bytes 31, 139, 0, 0) gave an uncoded, empty TypeError; the stream is now cancelled only while open and a failed gzip read is coded (astronomy.test.js, failing on the old code). scientific-image.ts: imageGrid gave an axis of several samples whose world transform put them all at one coordinate (FITS CDELT of 0) an invented unit extent, and took fractional, zero or missing dims; both now reject with coded errors (scientific-image.test.js, failing on the old code). reader-wire.ts and readers.worker.ts read: a class-instance argument with a read method crosses to the worker as its bound read alone, which is all a FileSource (openNetCDF, openVTK) uses; every other worker reader takes URLs, bytes, plain records or functions, and every reader result is a plain object, so no needed property is lost. render.ts: the frame uniform gave every layer the whole target's size, so in a multi-view frame pixel-sized lines, flow ribbons, graph marks and parallel-coordinate strokes were sized in the wrong pixels and the globe sky ray read @builtin(position) as if the view filled the target; the uniform now carries the view's size and its origin (render-frame.test.js, failing on the old code; sciviz, atmosphere, views and geo-vector GPU suites pass on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer). A singular camera matrix (a custom Camera.project that flattens depth) kept the previous frame's inverse in the frame uniform (zeros on a first frame), so volume, slice and sky rays were rebuilt for another camera; the pass now throws a RangeError linked to the errors page (render-frame.test.js, failing on the old code). query.ts Spreadsheet: the column headers were buttons with tabIndex -1 outside the grid's active-descendant model, so sorting was mouse only; ArrowUp from the first row now makes a header the active cell and Enter or Space sorts it, ascending then descending (query-browser.mjs, failing on the old code, passes on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer). projections.ts: a Lambert conformal conic whose latitudeOrigin was the cone's divergent pole rejected for a southern cone but not for a northern one, since tan(pi/2) is a finite 1.6e16 in doubles; climateProjection('lambert-conformal-conic', { latitudeOrigin: -90 }) returned a projection with a false northing near 4.6e18 m and now rejects like its mirror (projections.test.js, failing on the old code). provenance.ts read with no defect. probe.ts: quads were split into two triangles and interpolated linearly, so a 2D curvilinear image sampled differently from the regular image it copies (corner values 0, 1, 1, 0: 0 at the centre instead of the image's bilinear 0.5) and integrateGrid gave the regular 2D image's integral as 1/3 instead of 1/2; quads now locate by Gauss-Newton on their bilinear map and integrate with 3x3 Gauss points (probe.test.js, failing on the old code). progressive.ts, pointcloud.ts, pipeline.ts and pick.ts read with no defect. progressive.ts: a view that went away (removed from engine.render's views, its id never used again) kept its reduced colour and depth target for the controller's life, so a figure whose view ids change leaked GPU memory per id; per-view state now lives in the shared ViewStates and a view the previous frame did not draw releases at the next frame (progressive.test.js, failing on the old code). volume-depth.ts had the same per-view leak: the full-size colour and depth capture of a view that went away stayed until every volume was forgotten; it now uses the same ViewStates pruning (volume-depth.test.js, failing on the old code). pdf.ts: paint-order was ignored, so a shape or text whose SVG paints the stroke first (contourSVG's white-haloed level labels: stroke-width 4 on 12 px glyphs) was written fill-then-stroke and the halo covered the label; the stroke is now painted under the fill, inherited like the other painting properties (pdf.test.js, failing on the old code). parcoords.ts: the accessible controls() range inputs refreshed only on axis reorder or flip, so after a pointer brush, undo or a linked view's selection they still showed the old range and editing one endpoint brushed with the stale other one (brush period to [1, 20] by pointer, inputs still read the full domain); they now follow every selection change (parcoords-browser.mjs, failing on the old code, passes on WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer). pick-gpu.ts, pick-gpu-shaders.ts, parcoords-layer.ts and parcoords-density.ts read with no defect (the density segment choice checked exhaustively for 2 to 40 axes and up to 300 pixels). pick-gpu.ts and density.ts: a pick or readCounts timeout above 2^31-1 ms fired after 1 ms in Node (its timer overflows) and one above 2^53-1 made GpuPicking.pick throw synchronously instead of rejecting; both now reject with a coded RangeError for a timeout outside (0, 2147483647] ms (pick-gpu.test.js, density.test.js, failing on the old code). openfoam.ts: a binary field whose internal values held a quote or comment byte failed with an unterminated string (the boundaryField search tokenized the raw bytes), a boundaryField with a `#includeEtc` directive failed to read, and quoted pattern keys, patch-group keys and `$` macro values were ignored so a patch silently took the owner cell value; the field file is now indexed once binary-safely and entries resolve by OpenFOAM's name, group, pattern precedence (engineering.test.js, failing on the old code). omezarr.ts (re-exports only) read, no defect. ometiff.ts: image() looked up the origin Plane by sample index instead of channel index (an RGB channel's second and third samples got origin 0), added a Plane Position in mm or Å to coordinates in the PhysicalSize unit unconverted, and a TiffData with neither IFD nor PlaneCount naming a companion file took its plane count from the main file, so planes past it were unmapped; all three fixed (astronomy.test.js, failing on the old code). notebook.ts: render() resolved with a dispose function when its signal aborted during the first load (the view was already torn down); it now rejects with the abort reason (notebook-lifecycle.test.js, failing on the old code). netcdf.ts: unpacking took -127 as missing in a byte variable with no _FillValue, which the NetCDF Users Guide (NUG) attribute conventions forbid, ignored the NUG rule that the fill bounds the valid range when no valid_* attribute is set, and ignored `_Unsigned = "true"` (NUG best practices), so a stored byte 255 read -1; byte has no default fill now, the explicit or default fill bounds the valid range (1 away for integers, 2 ulps for floats), and _Unsigned reinterprets byte, short and int data plus the _FillValue, missing_value and valid_* attributes stored in the variable's type (climate-formats.test.js, failing on the old code). monitor.ts and monitor-sweep.ts: installFigureStats returned a disposed monitor again, so a figure whose monitor was disposed could never be measured; a key with a null prototype tracked without a name crashed snapshot() with a TypeError; sweepLimits accepted a timeoutMs above 2147483647, which hosts fire after 1 ms, so every size failed as timed out; a disposed monitor is replaced on the next install, such a key is named 'object', and the long deadline rejects with a coded RangeError (monitor.test.js, failing on the old code). multivolume.ts, molecule-render.ts and molecule-data.ts read again, no new defect (the impostor normal uses the inverse transpose, the CPU pick matches the shader's closed sphere and cylinder surfaces, and the PDB loader supplies the element when its column is empty). mrc.ts, three open questions settled from Cheng et al. 2015 (MRC2014, J. Struct. Biol. 192:146-150, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4642651/, read in full), the CCP-EM MRC2014 page (https://www.ccpem.ac.uk/mrc-format/mrc2014/) and IMOD's mrc_format.txt (https://bio3d.colorado.edu/imod/doc/mrc_format.txt), both fetched 2026-11-16: mode 101 nibble order was already right (lower coordinate in the low nibble); world() added ORIGIN and the starts, which place the same first sample, so a subvolume landed twice its offset away, and it added a complex map's phase origin in pixels as if it were angstroms; IMOD-stamped files with flag 1 clear (unsigned bytes) read mode 0 signed and flag 4 clear (negated origin) kept the wrong sign. All four fixed (astronomy.test.js, failing on the old code); what the sources leave open is in limits.md. Closure pass: the files no earlier pass named were read whole: series.ts and tensor.ts (re-exports), aggregate-types.ts (types), cell-complex.ts (pulling triangulation as cited in the file, Beck and Sanyal 2018 section 5.7, paper not refetched: each 3D cell is coned from its smallest global point ID over the fans of the faces that miss it, so neighbours agree on shared faces), labels.ts, link.ts and math.ts, no defect; formats.ts lines 1 to 55 and 250 to 1150 (NIfTI-1 and NIfTI-2 offsets, qform quaternion, DICOM explicit and implicit VR, UN, sequences, File Meta, Bits Stored, Modality LUT, planar and YBR_FULL_422) and a11y.ts lines 1 to 40 and 828 to 833, read whole; fallback.wasm and flow.wasm are the built Rust kernels already read, with their size records. Two defects: cf-time.ts decode threw 'invalid datetime' for a value a hair before the reference instant (seconds since 2000-01-01 at -1e-12 left the remainder at exactly 86400 s, hour 24), now normalised to 0 <= remainder < 86400 (cf.test.js, failing on the old code); dicomSeries stacked a slice of another plane or pixel grid in the same series (a scout or reformat) with the first slice's orientation and spacing, and now refuses it with a coded error (formats-spec.test.js, failing on the old code). A follow-up: dicomSeries also rejects a missing slice (a step more than 1.5 times the median; 3D Slicer's DICOM loader warns on such a series as not equally spaced, read from its forum threads, the user guide page not fetched) unless allowGaps stacks it and lists the gaps (formats-spec.test.js, failing on the old code). MRC ORIGIN keeps sample 0 at ORIGIN, not half a sample in from the corner the paper names; the reason is in limits.md. nrrd.ts read, no defect found. query.ts: series() over an int64 or categorical column silently dropped every row and returned an empty series; it now rejects with a coded error (query.test.js, failing on the old code). provenance-containers.ts: readPNGProvenance cancelled an already failed inflate stream, so a damaged compressed record gave an empty uncoded TypeError, and a record that was not UTF-8 JSON gave a bare SyntaxError or TypeError (readSVGProvenance too); both are coded (provenance.test.js, failing on the old code). resources.ts, publication-labels.ts, provenance-versions.ts, progressive-policy.ts, plot-window.ts and pick-visibility.ts read, no defect found. | Nine GPU volume defects are in known-bugs with repros (absorption per box unit not physical length, voxel-centre offset, view direction through the normal matrix, two meanings of density, the quality wrapper with a colorScale map, jittered preintegration span, preintegration range, multivolume culling and depth, cyclic table offset); HDF5 dense attributes (message 21) not read; HDF5 implicit chunk index walks every chunk; most of the package not re-read; no mutation score |
| ifc (every row of the gap table below matched by 2026-11-16, against the web BIM libraries. Every source file read line by line: index.ts, open.ts, data.ts, units.ts, query.ts, step.ts, worker.ts, ifc.worker.ts, bcf.ts, ids.ts, tolerance.ts, schema.ts, write.ts, raw.ts; the /write sources are in docs/clean-room.md, "IFC property write-back") | buildingSMART IFC 4.3 ADD2 documentation, read from its source text (https://github.com/buildingSMART/IFC4.3.x-development, docs/schemas): IfcMapConversion (scale, anticlockwise rotation atan2(XAxisOrdinate, XAxisAbscissa), translation), IfcSIUnit, IfcConversionBasedUnit (Table 1 values), IfcConversionBasedUnitWithOffset (the offset is added after the factor, so SI = (v - offset) x factor); BIPM, The International System of Units, 9th edition, 2019, section 3 (a prefix applies to the unit before its power: mm2 = 10^-6 m2); buildingSMART BCF-XML 3.0 Documentation/README.md and Schemas/visinfo.xsd (https://github.com/buildingSMART/BCF-XML, release_3_0); buildingSMART IDS 1.0 user manual (specifications, property, entity, partOf, material, attribute, classification facets, restrictions, units), Schema/ids.xsd and implementers' documentation (developer guide, tolerance, DataTypes, IFC2X3 occurrence-type mapping table) (https://github.com/buildingSMART/IDS, development at 870f9c4e6; test data: its 334 TestCases pairs, CC BY-ND 4.0, kept verbatim); M. Johansson, "Integrating Occlusion Culling and Hardware Instancing for Efficient Real-time Rendering of Building Information Models", GRAPP 2013, https://www.scitepress.org/Papers/2013/43028/43028.pdf. Open: the IFC 4.3 HTML pages on standards.buildingsmart.org refused automated fetches, so the same text was read from the documentation repository; the buildingSMART Australia georeferencing guide (ePSet_MapConversion) was not fetched and its property names come from the IFC2x3 files that use them | Units: every property and quantity value converted to SI by the project's IfcUnitAssignment (prefixed, conversion based, with offset, derived); georeference both ways against the formula; spatial tree and relations against the schema's relationship attributes; type property inheritance (occurrence overrides type, IFC4 IfcRelDefinesByType); two hand-written fixtures (IFC4 in millimetres, IFC2X3 in feet) through real web-ifc 0.0.78 | Before this pass the package returned geometry and product IDs only: property sets, relations, units and georeferencing were not read | /data entry: readIFCData, propertiesOf, materialsOf, classificationsOf, modelToMap, mapToModel; /bcf entry: readBCF, writeBCF, parseBCFViewpoint, writeBCFViewpoint, viewpointFromBCFJSON, viewpointToBCFJSON, viewFromBCF, bcfFromView, newBCFGuid (sources: BCF-XML release_3_0 and release_2_1 Schemas visinfo.xsd, markup.xsd, extensions.xsd, project.xsd, documents.xsd, version.xsd and Documentation/README.md; BCF-API release_3_0 Schemas/Collaboration/Viewpoint/*.json, https://github.com/buildingSMART/BCF-API; test data: BCF-XML Test Cases, CC BY-ND 4.0); /ids entry: parseIDS, writeIDS, checkIDS, bcfTopicsFromIDS; /query entry: findElements (class filters match subtypes by the IFC4 or IFC4X3 inheritance read from the buildingSMART EXPRESS schemas IFC4.exp ADD2 TC1 and IFC4X3_ADD2.exp, https://github.com/buildingSMART/Standards-builds; no IFC2X3 tree ships because the IFC2X3 TC1 file reserves all rights; readIFCData records an IFC2X3 model's inheritance from the open web-ifc runtime, one inherited GetLineIDsWithType lookup per schema entity type, so IFC2X3-only classes match their subtypes, docs/limits.md), groupElements, effectivePredefinedType, ancestorsOf, descendantsOf, isSubtypeOf, subtypesOf, supertypesOf; root: openIFC, readIFCGeometry with float64 origin rebasing | The gap table below |

### ifc gap table (2026-11-16)

Rivals, from their public documentation and feature lists only: That Open Engine components and fragments
(docs.thatopen.com), xeokit SDK (xeokit.io), web-ifc-viewer of the IFC.js era. "Matched" means Margo does all the rival
does for that feature; the package stays "partial" until every row is matched.

| Feature | Best rival | Margo status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Geometry with BIM IDs per element | That Open IfcLoader, xeokit | Matched: `parseIFC`, `readIFCGeometry` |
| Load in a worker with a deadline | That Open (worker conversion) | Matched: `/worker` `parseIFCInWorker` |
| Property sets, quantities, type properties, materials and layers, classifications | That Open IfcRelationsIndexer, xeokit MetaModel | Matched: `/data`; beyond the rivals, every measure is converted to SI with its raw value kept |
| Spatial structure tree with element containment | xeokit TreeViewPlugin, That Open classifier by spatial structure | Matched: `/data` `spatial`, `storey` per element |
| Units to meters, georeferencing (IfcMapConversion, IFC2x3 ePSet_MapConversion, IfcSite, true north), origin rebasing | xeokit double precision, That Open coordinate to origin | Matched: `/data` georeference and conversions, `readIFCGeometry` `origin` |
| Find and classify by class (subtypes included), type, storey, property query | That Open Classifier and ItemsFinder | Matched: `/query` `findElements` (class filters follow each schema's IFC inheritance, `subtypes: false` for exact classes), `groupElements`, `descendantsOf`, `isSubtypeOf`, `subtypesOf`, `supertypesOf`; beyond the rivals, numeric conditions compare in SI with the IDS tolerance |
| Per-element visibility, isolate, hide, ghost, colour override | That Open Hider and Highlighter, xeokit X-ray and colorize | Matched: `/scene` `hide`, `show`, `isolate` (hiding or ghosting the rest), `setGhosted` (translucent x-ray copies), `setColor`, `setOpacity`, `select`, `stateOf`; checked on native WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer (tests/scene-gpu-check.mjs) |
| Instancing of repeated IfcMappedItem geometry and merged batches with per-element ID maps for picking | That Open Fragments, xeokit | Matched: `/scene` `readIFCMeshes` reads each geometryExpressID once, `createIFCScene` instances geometry placed twice or more and batches the rest (BatchedMesh, one draw per batch), `elementAt` maps an instance index or the batch's UV0 element slot to the expressID |
| Streaming load with progress (async iterator, AbortSignal) | That Open Fragments, xeokit XKT | Matched: `/scene` `streamIFCMeshes` is an async iterator of chunks (placements plus only the geometries new to the stream) with `loaded`/`total` progress that grows every chunk and ends complete, an AbortSignal, `elements` to stream a subset (one storey at a time) and `order: 'envelope'` (shell classes first) for a recognisable first frame; `/worker` `streamIFCInWorker` streams the same chunks from a worker with moved buffers and terminates it on break, abort or silence; `IFCScene.add` joins chunks into the existing instance groups (batched parts move in when a geometry crosses `instancing`) and grows batches instead of opening one per chunk, so the streamed scene draws and picks exactly as a whole load (tests/stream.test.js on the 640 KB buildingSMART Architectural.ifc in 8-element chunks; tests/scene-gpu-check.mjs pixel-equal on native WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer). Sources (full text fetched; their loading and streaming sections read, not every section): Hong-Lei Lu, Jia-Xing Wu, Yu-Shen Liu, Wan-Qi Wang, Dynamically loading IFC models on a web browser based on spatial semantic partitioning, Visual Computing for Industry, Biomedicine, and Art 2:4 (2019), https://doi.org/10.1186/s42492-019-0011-z (load by storey on demand: `elements`); Xiaojun Liu, Ning Xie, Kai Tang, Jinyuan Jia, Lightweighting for Web3D visualization of large-scale BIM scenes in real-time, Graphical Models 88 (2016), https://doi.org/10.1016/j.gmod.2016.06.001 (exterior products first, progressive loading: `order: 'envelope'`); Timothy Scully, Sebastian Friston, Carmen Fan, Jozef Doboš, Anthony Steed, glTF streaming from 3D Repo to X3DOM, Web3D 2016, https://doi.org/10.1145/2945292.2945297 (multipart supermeshes fewer draws while streaming: batches that grow). Unread: That Open Fragments and xeokit XKT internals (their public feature lists only) |
| Storey plan views and section clipping | xeokit StoreyViewsPlugin and SectionPlanesPlugin, That Open Clipper and Plans | Matched: `/scene` `clippingPlanes` (up to 16 on every material, materials' limit raised from 6; the first 8 cut in the rasterizer), `sectionBox` and `storeyPlan` (isolate, cut, orthographic top camera), BCF section planes through `applyBCFView`; `/section` `IFCSectionView` caps every cut per element (element or fixed colour) and draws the cut edges through `@margoengine/lines`, both trimmed by the other planes, and `sectionIFC` returns the caps, edges and closed section loops per element and plane for plan drawings and exports; checked on native WebGPU and the WebGL2 layer (tests/scene-gpu-check.mjs, nine planes). Sources: R. Minetto, N. Volpato, J. Stolfi, R. Gregori, M. da Silva, "An optimal algorithm for 3D triangle mesh slicing", Computer-Aided Design 92 (2017) 1-10, https://www.inf.ufpr.br/murilo/public/CAD-slicing.pdf (read: hash-table contour construction, the vertex-on-plane rule, open chains of non-manifold input); the W3C WGSL specification (clip_distances holds at most 8). Open, not read: J. Rossignac, A. Megahed, B. Schneider, "Interactive inspection of solids: cross-sections and interferences", SIGGRAPH 1992, doi:10.1145/133994.134092 (abstract only: GPU stencil capping, not used because it needs closed solids and one ordered pass per plane and yields no polygons); I. Sutherland, G. Hodgman, "Reentrant polygon clipping", CACM 17(1), 1974 (the half-space clipping is the textbook step, from memory) |
| BCF 3.0 viewpoints import and export | xeokit BCFViewpointsPlugin, That Open BCFTopics | Matched: `/bcf` reads BCF 3.0, 2.1, 2.0 and 1.0 archives and viewpoints (topics, comments, documents, snippets, related topics, snapshots, bitmaps) and writes 3.0 or 2.1; BCF-API 3.0 JSON viewpoints both ways; `viewFromBCF` and `bcfFromView` map camera, visibility (hints and exceptions in BCF order), selection, colour, section planes and lines to engine axes and expressIDs, decomposed containers applied to their parts. Checked against ten buildingSMART test cases on their Architectural.ifc |
| IDS 1.0 checking | That Open IDSSpecifications | Matched: `/ids` `parseIDS`, `writeIDS`, `checkIDS` agree with all 334 official IDS 1.0 test cases (every facet, restriction and tolerance case); beyond the rival, any IFC entity can be checked (materials, predefined property sets, the project), each failure says why in words, and `bcfTopicsFromIDS` turns failures into BCF topics |
| Write changed property values back to IFC | That Open (IFC editing and export) | Matched: `/write` `editIFC` sets, adds and removes single, list and enumerated property values with any measure type and unit, whole property sets and quantities (IfcElementQuantity, every simple quantity), renames and sets attributes; `saveIFC` saves through SaveModel with an AbortSignal. Beyond the rival: shared sets, relationships and properties are copied on write so an edit changes only its element, new IfcRoot entities get new GlobalIds and ADDED owner histories and changed ones MODIFIED histories that keep the owner, values are given in SI and written in project units, and web-ifc's exponent reals are made ISO 10303-21 conformant. Round trips re-read with `readIFCData` and reopened in a fresh web-ifc in tests/write.test.js (IFC4 and IFC2X3) |
